ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u25	Thank you very much. Mr Marek Zagórski is responsible for digitization.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u26	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! In fact, I confirm, the Operational Programme of Poland digital, and especially its first axis, is extremely important for the economic development of Poland, the development of civilization, because we are talking here about the construction of this infrastructure element, which will constitute the modernity of the Polish economy and access to modern technologies in the best possible way, i.e. on the basis of a fiber optic network. The investments we are making are at the moment the biggest investments in Europe. The value of the entire programme is over PLN 6 billion, of which the funding under the OP PC programme is PLN 4 billion. The programme assumes in principle the elimination of white spots. The elimination of white spots, i.e. the elimination of areas for which we have identified an impossibility, the willingness over the coming years to invest in the fibre network by telecommunications operators. This programme is implemented on the basis of special research carried out and as a result we have divided Poland into several dozen competition areas, where we have selected telecommunications operators who make investments from funding in various stages. Thus, in the first and second competitions, the first one of its nature was of very little importance, the second competition was important for us, and we selected the operators who set up investments that assume the coverage of 1 036 540 households and the connection to the high-speed Internet of 7900 educational establishments, and the amount of funding for projects in the first and second competition is over PLN 2200 million. It is still worth noting, because it is such an element that differs this project from previous investments under the so-called. regional broadband networks in the previous programming period, that we are talking about investments made by telecom operators and not by local authorities in that case. Investments therefore go directly to consumers and are therefore effective. At the same time, we decided to ensure the even distribution of this fibre network across Poland by obliging all beneficiaries to connect all educational institutions to this network. This will result in an even distribution of this network of at least 20,000. location of schools, and this also gives us the opportunity to carry out another project, which is the National Education Network. As far as Lublin Voivodeship is concerned, the problem in Lublin Voivodeship was indeed one of the biggest, because none of the operators really wanted to invest there. Not only did he not want to invest commercially, on a larger scale, but he did not want to invest in the first and second competitions. Only in the third contest, when we changed the rules, did we divide Lubelskie Voivodeship into 12... 13 areas, because one area is shared with the Mazowieckie voivodship - such a Radom sub-area - we have selected contractors for all competition areas. With all contractors already signed contracts and value, the results of this project will be the following. Well, almost 700,000 will be attached. households. The value of this project on the scale of Lublin Voivodeship in this combined area will be PLN 1143 million, of which public funding will amount to 750 million. This is, as you will find out, the biggest investment under the Operational Programme in Poland digital, the biggest investment in the fibre network will be in Lublin Voivodeship. As part of this project, as I said, all schools in the Lublin Voivodeship which do not yet have access to broadband internet will be connected. The duration of this project, each of these projects, is 36 months, but one of the main indicators is the connection of 80% educational establishments within 18 months, so we can say that within the period from today, within 2 years all educational establishments will be connected. It can also be said effectively that every third household in the Lublin Voivodeship will be connected to the high-speed network as part of this project. This is the scale of these investments. We believe that this will really be a very big impulse for the whole of Poland, but above all for the Lublin Voivodeship. We expect the effects to be visible in the coming months. Now the process of preparing these investments is underway, but I think that within 12 months we will have the first results in the form of connected households and connected schools. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u33	Thank you very much. Mr Paweł Cybulski corresponds to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u34	High Izbo! Dear Marshal, I would like to answer this question, which has practically appeared for a long time. All these doubts that the opposition raises regarding the operation of the National Tax Administration are directed in such a special way, such a distrustful one, indicating that something really bad is happening, something dangerous, when it comes to entrepreneurs and about what is happening in the National Tax Administration. I wanted to say first of all, to ensure that we carry out tasks based on the National Tax Administration Act, based on tax laws and all tasks we carry out primarily on the basis of law. Second thing. We are also implementing the latest legal provisions related to business law related to the constitution for business, and we are fully aware that we are primarily meant to serve honest entrepreneurs and reduce their risk of doing business. So our actions are directed towards what is clearly written in the assumptions of the National Tax Administration - we can read it, read it - that the National Tax Administration is primarily intended to assist honest entrepreneurs in their activities, secondly, to fight against the grey and black zone, thirdly, to protect the borders of the European Union. And that's what we do. Of course, there's a question about Kielce. In fact, it is a question that may have resulted from misunderstood or ripped from the context of certain actions of the Director of the Board of Tax Administration in Kielce. I think you already know that, because we have repeatedly responded to various kinds of interpellations, questions of the State in this matter. I would like to say once again that the number of tax checks is, of course, falling and I suspect it will be falling. The task of tax controls is not to collect extra money, just as some people here are trying to prove. The purpose of tax control is to increase tax discipline among taxpayers and, above all, to bring about voluntary tax clearance. That's basic. Here we don't reach into the pocket for extra money, we don't want to chase honest taxpayers. First of all, we want these controls to be as low as possible, we want to implement the principles of the business constitution, we want entrepreneurs to act in the most convenient way, as safe as possible, so that they have a good economic environment, so that they feel safe among their competitors, who are not always honest, apply various types of tax fraud, which makes them more attractive on the market. As the National Tax Administration within our powers and capabilities, we want to bring about, we bring about the reality of economic life. Why did I say it's a good thing that these controls and safeguards are less that these restrictive measures are less? This is because we have focused above all on analytical activities. You know that we are introducing a new kind of legislative solution. We've introduced sealing packages, fuel packs, transport monitors, SENT, and we've also introduced the STIR Act. All economic links, all economic activities are analysed using the JPK analytical system. Therefore, we move away from such hard controls. As I said before, we want to make life as difficult as possible for taxpayers. And if from any of the writings that come to you, you read the signals saying that something bad is happening, then I say it is not true. It's out of context. These are some sentences ripped out of context that you sometimes try to manipulate or intimidate entrepreneurs and say that the situation is very bad. I stress that with regard to the operation of the National Tax Administration, the situation is good and goes in the right direction. There's no pathology here. If there are any isolated cases, we react quickly to this type of action. As far as safeguards are concerned, I would just like to remind you that, for example, in 2014 and 2015, these safeguards were actually very much, but they were safeguards for poles, for non-existent entities. The pillars were treated actually the same as the taxpayers. They were sent to the poles. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Calls, and it's known that criminals don't respond to calls, so it was hard to talk about tax collection here. Indeed, the collection of this tax was low, even fatal, because only 1 million could be obtained out of 7 million secured tax revenues in 2015. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u48	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The task of building the S12 road is included in the National Road Construction Programme, but without funding. It should therefore be stressed here that the preparatory work is currently being carried out in order to identify the optimal preferred route option. This phase has been in progress since September 2017 and will continue until mid-2020. Designers working on behalf of the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways, as with all such agreements, prepare possible variants of the course, presenting them to local authorities and the public during information meetings. The Ministry of Infrastructure does not interfere with the selection process or the detailed results of analyses, these are complex, very specialised issues concerning the design of environmental conditions, economic analyses, etc. It should be made clear that at this stage of the investment process no decision has been taken on the choice of the option of the S12 route. In advance of the questions, I will say at once: none, not total, not partial, nor foreboding - none. It is completely unfounded to say that at this stage any choice has been made. The process of road design is still ongoing, with two tours of information meetings with residents and actors from the areas of the municipalities through which this road will take place. With regard to the raised issue of changing the option of the road path, I would like to stress that nothing like this has happened. In the course of the work, various options of the road are being developed and considered, which are then presented to the public at information meetings. However, I would like to stress strongly once again that none of the options that have been developed and presented so far is yet to be the final preferred option. They are all developed with the same accuracy, care and degree of detail. The selection of the proposed courses was influenced mainly by protests of the inhabitants of Kowali, Gozdu and Skarishev. For this reason, the company preparing the project materials is still working on further variants of the road, taking into account the needs and conclusions of the residents. Once again, I strongly stress that all these options are equivalent at this stage of the investment process. I would like to say one more thing: that the option will be chosen by the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways does not prejudge the fact that it will be recognised by the Regional Environment Director. The option shall be indicated in the decision issued by the Ministry of Economic Affairs following the administrative procedure, including on the basis of an analysis of the material provided by the investor and taking into account the public consultation process. I will immediately say that it has also happened that the option finally adopted by WDOŚ was quite different than that presented as recommended by the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways. In conclusion, the final route of the S12 road around Radom will be indicated by the Regional Environment Director by administrative means after all necessary documents have been produced by an independent company and accepted by the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways. With regard to the issue that you have mentioned, the ownership of land near which the S12 road will eventually take place, I would like to inform you that the issue of ownership of parcels, particularly private land, is not taken into account at all at the stage of designing the investment process. During the preparatory work phase, they are not carefully examined, identified specific properties that will possibly be necessary in the future to carry out the task. This is related, among other things. the fact that several options are currently being considered, and that work at this stage is carried out on materials with less detail. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u63	Thank you very much. Mr Marek Chodkiewicz, Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Infrastructure, answers.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u64	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! At the beginning of my speech I would like to recall this number: the number of kilometres of local roads is 374 thousand. It's a colossal number. The analysis of the 2 years of implementation of the municipal and district road infrastructure development programme in 2016 and 2017 shows that the funding from the state budget for the construction, reconstruction and renovation of district and municipal roads was significantly lower than the needs. The demand for funding was about 100% greater than the limits available. Given the limited financial capacity of local government units, it is not possible for them to involve more of their own resources and there is therefore a need to support them through a financial mechanism established at national level. Therefore, the Council of Ministers submitted to the Sejm a draft law on the Fund for the Roads of Local Government, which provides for the creation of a new support mechanism for local government units carrying out investments on public roads. The proposed changes in the way in which public road tasks managed by local government units are financed are designed to provide more financial resources together with ensuring their stability at the statutory level, as well as to increase flexibility in the spending of these funds through the abandonment of annual planning and the possibility of financing multi-annual tasks. The Self-Government Road Fund, please of the High House, will gather unprecedented measures to finance the actions taken by the local authorities. In 2019 it will be over PLN 6 billion. The fund will finance the construction, reconstruction and renovation of district and municipal roads, as well as the financing of tasks involving the construction of local bridges located in local roads. In addition, the Fund will finance investments on local roads, which were also considered relevant due to the defence of the Republic of Poland. Importantly, the funds raised by the Fund will allow for the financing of investments which were still eligible for support in 2018 under the rules laid down in the current municipal development programme and district road infrastructure. The mechanism will therefore be changed smoothly. It should be added that new rules will be introduced to grant subsidies under which multi-annual investment will be supported and that the value of the grant will be up to 30 million for one investment against the currently existing 3 million. The amount of funding from the fund for district and municipal tasks will also depend on the revenue of the local government unit concerned, applying the principle that the lower the own income of the entity concerned, the greater the value of the funding. It should also be noted that there is a change that this funding could be 80%. the value of the investment. So far, according to the current rules, it was 50-50, 50%. Some local authorities could not participate in this process because they could not find this 50% financing of their investments. Increasing the level of co-financing will allow the implementation of tasks in municipalities and poorer districts, which with 50% co-financing Like I said, they couldn't get it. The creation of the fund also aims to improve road safety, the technical parameters of the local road network, as well as to improve and increase the attractiveness and accessibility of investment areas. The Act also provides for support for the construction of bridges under which the local government units will be allocated funds to cover the preparation of investments under the programme of the Bridges for the regions. In order to encourage local authorities to implement bridge investments, which are important not only for the region but also for the whole country, the funding of these tasks will also cover 80% in any case. the cost of implementation, including access roads, so as to constitute a whole. The proposed law also aims to ensure stable financing of voivodship, district and municipal roads of defence importance. The development of road infrastructure will allow for the smooth adoption of allied forces to strengthen NATO in the territory of the Republic of Poland and will contribute to the maintenance and development of operational capabilities of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. Ladies and gentlemen, the Minister of Infrastructure has proposed solutions that provide financial support to local governments of an unprecedented scale, especially to help poorer local authorities, contributing to equal opportunities for development and living standards of all Polish residents. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

These improvements consisted, first of all, in the fact that we quickly mobilised the resources needed to implement or cover the implementation costs of the programme by local government units. The first funds went to local government units at the end of June. Secondly, it was also important that we determined the cost necessary to handle a single claim for benefit under the "Good start' scheme. We estimated this cost of PLN 10 per child, but an important issue which also had an impact on the incentive aspect was that in the Council of Ministers regulation, which defined the conditions, the way the programme was implemented, we determined that at least 80% the amount allocated to the costs of implementing the programme should be allocated to remuneration and other benefits for persons directly involved in the implementation of the programme. Other activities include: the development of a special manual for local authorities containing a detailed procedure for the implementation of this programme, a very rapid development of a model application that appeared on the pages of the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy, in the Public Information Bulletin. It was very important to disseminate the electronic communication channel between applicants and local government units, which were responsible for examining claims for benefits and payment of benefits. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u108	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Mrs. Member! Thank you very much for this question, because the treatment of strokes is indeed one of the priorities of the Ministry of Health and this is an area where time and the use of state-of-the-art technologies in the treatment of this type of disease play a major role. Mechanical Trombectomy guarantees even twice the effectiveness of existing therapies. Therefore, the Ministry of Health is examining the draft pilot scheme regulation, which will enter into force on 1 December 2018, and which will last for 2 years. This programme will allocate at least PLN 100 million to finance treatment with this method - mechanical Trombectomy. This piloting is intended to carefully examine the effectiveness of this method before the final decision to include it in the basket of guaranteed benefits. It is worth saying that this is a procedure that is positively assessed by a national consultant in the field of neurology. Therefore, we are implementing this pilot programme and in at least seven centres throughout Poland, in many regions of the country, including among others in Silesia, this piloting will be carried out. Our goal is first of all to examine the effectiveness carefully, because we have promising signals about the fact that it is very high, but we want to examine it carefully and then evaluate it so that we can develop precise criteria for qualification for this procedure, so that we can allocate money as efficiently as possible in these extremely difficult situations for patients. So, to sum up, it's December 1st that we're gonna start piloting. This flight will take two years. Within this framework, at least PLN 100 million will be transferred from the National Health Fund on the basis of this statutory mandate to carry out pilotage. In our view, if everything is done as intended, if the results of this pilotage are as expected, it will be possible to include this in the basket of guaranteed benefits. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u118	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! The procedure covers 20 groups of operators, 20 groups of fruit and vegetables producers. I'm not going to mention here the names of those entities that are the subject of an investigation. These investigations are conducted in the District Attorney's Office and the Regional Attorney's Office. As of today, ladies and gentlemen, the charges in four investigations have been brought to a total of 60 people. The object of the charges was almost PLN 400 million, and all four proceedings together concern grants for PLN 1480 million. As the Minister mentioned here, the proceedings concern groups of fruit and vegetable producers. During the proceedings the heaviest modus operandi was established. The condition for receiving the funding was that the local marshal of the voivodship should decide on the preliminary recognition of producers as a group of fruit and vegetables producers. Certain conditions had to be met for such decisions, namely the submission of a 5-year investigation plan to recognise membership of the producer and fruit group. The group, which wanted to be considered as such a group of fruit and vegetable producers, had to indicate in the submitted plan what investments it intends to make in the form of purchasing machinery, real estate or infrastructure elements, and carry out these investments. These documents provided false data related to the group's investment plans and its financial, production and technology capabilities. These plans were to be subject to regular checks. These checks were to be carried out by marshals' offices. In the course of the investigation, it was found that these checks were often superficial. [[Horrible.]] A large difference is also noted in the application to the European Commission for a refund. For national programmes, this represents 22% allocation and regional operational programmes - only 15%. The progress of some regional operational programmes is so worrying that even the European Commission has covered some of them - I mean Lubelskie, Świętokrzyskie, Podlaskie and Warmia-Mazurskie - special supervision with special expert support. Weaker results in regional operational programmes represent a real threat to our country's loss of funds as a result of the so-called "use." rules n+3. At present, this threat concerns regional programmes. For national programmes, the situation is satisfactory. It welcomes the fact that the Ministry of Investment and Development's actions aimed at mobilising regions for more efficient and more efficient spending of money under regional operational programmes and the rescue of threatened funds estimated at the end of August at more than EUR 1 billion are delivering results. Unfortunately, there are still regions where there are real risks associated with the loss of the funds I mentioned. Taking into account the application of rule n+3, according to data on 30 September this year. for regional operational programmes, the amount of €108 million is still at stake. This situation concerns five voivodships: Podlasie Voivodship - EUR 34 million, Warmia-Mazurskie Voivodship - EUR 29 million, Świętokrzyskie Voivodship - EUR 22 million, as well as Śląskie Voivodship - EUR 13 million and Lesser Poland - nearly EUR 10 million. On the other hand, with regard to the Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship, I would like to stress that following the intervention of the Minister for Development of Mr Jerzy Kwieciński, the data as at 30 September indicate that the risk of losing funds under rule n+3 has been eliminated, but this is one of the lowest regions in terms of regional operational programmes, contracting levels - only 51% of the available allocation resources, and one of the lowest levels of request for a refund to the European Commission - only 14%. These levels are much lower than the average for all programmes and the average for regional operational programmes, and it is therefore necessary to step up management activities and improve the implementation of this programme. Analysis of the implementation framework of the regional operational programme at the end of the second quarter of this year indicated that more than EUR 1 billion was at stake. However, in the case of the Regional Operational Programme of Kujawsko-Pomorskie Voivodeship, this was unfortunately the entire amount of the performance reserve, i.e. over EUR 114 million. The Ministry expects that in the near future we will learn directly from the detailed reports from the individual managing authorities what it looks like in detail. The main factors which, according to the opinion of the Ministry of Investment and Development, have affected delays in the implementation of the Kujawsko-Pomorskie RPO are the following: too late the mobilisation of funds for activities in certain thematic areas, such as ICT, too late the adoption of project selection criteria, which has consequently delayed the publication of competitions, and potential beneficiaries have therefore not had a chance to get familiar with the conditions for individual calls sufficiently early. Ladies and gentlemen! In order to minimise possible delays, the Ministry of Investment and Development as an institution for coordinating the implementation of the Partnership Agreement for 16 regional operational programmes shall undertake a number of actions to support both the programme and the implementation institutions of regional operational programmes. It belongs to them, among others. the obligation of RPO managing authorities to prepare corrective plans to reduce delays in meeting the targets set out in the 2018 programmes under the implementation framework mechanism. The implementation of these objectives will give rise to the allocation of implementation reserve measures, i.e. the currently frozen part of the allocation for the programme, which represents 6% in 2019. allocation for each programme. It is worth noting that the experts of the Ministry of Investment and Development took an active part in the process of renegotiating regional operational programmes and agreed with the European Commission in particular on horizontal issues, but also on individual programmes. In addition, of course, to align the programmes with the Responsible Development Strategy, the objective of changing regional operational programmes was to minimise the implementation risks associated with the delay in the use of European funds or the risk of their full use. In the process of renegotiating, the European Commission has been able to agree on solutions that are beneficial for the regions, among others. allow financing of project documentation for railway investments. The RPO of the Kujawsko-Pomorskie Voivodeship also carried out an extremely important relocation of funds. These actions have allowed for the redirection of measures from axes where implementation problems have been identified to axes of greater interest to potential beneficiaries. During the renegotiation of operational programmes, the values of the performance framework indicators were adjusted in justified cases. The correction of the target values of the meters established as milestones for 2018 is not the only facilitation. In addition, there has been liberalisation of the rules for the clearance of performance targets, which was introduced by the European Commission and promoted by the Ministry of Investment and Development. In line with the new rules, the basis for determining the value of the output indicator for the performance framework should be the data provided by the beneficiaries in the final payment applications. It has also been made possible to emphasise that the managing authority has taken into account data from indirect payment applications, provided that it can be confirmed that the project to achieve the products has been fully implemented. Of course, in addition to horizontal actions, the Ministry of Investment and Development also conducts a number of initiatives aimed directly at the beneficiaries of European funds, including, among others. State aid training for R &amp; D activities for representatives of scientific bodies planning or involved in projects co-financed by the Structural Funds. It is also worth noting here that the Ministry of Investment and Development also coordinates the European Commission's initiative for developing regions called Catching-up Regions. The Kujawsko-Pomorskie region is involved in the third edition of the initiative, where World Bank experts focus on the analysis of the area of aid for seniors in Grudziądz and entrepreneurship in Włocławek. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u214	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Generals! I have the honour to present the position of the Club of Law and Justice on the draft law amending the Act on the Office of the Minister of National Defence and the Act on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland. This is a very important act, the aim of which is to introduce solutions which will simplify the system of management and command of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland, as well as create conditions and organizational structures that will function during peace in a way that is as close to wartime. They will ensure a uniform division of competences and tasks and a clear separation of command levels in the Polish Army. The proposed amendments to the laws will also adapt the current command system of the Polish Armed Forces to the solutions adopted in NATO. In recent years there have been changes in the international environment, which have a direct impact on the security of the Republic of Poland. This has contributed, among other things. to significantly increase spending on our Armed Forces and upgrade equipment. Nevertheless, in order for the modern Armed Forces to function efficiently, it is necessary to construct the chain of command in a way that ensures a clear definition of the roles of the different levels and levels of command. Analysis of the existing Armed Forces control and command system leads to the conclusion that current command system solutions, e.g. The combination of strategic and operational levels in the national chain of command is not compatible with NATO and most of the Alliance countries. Within NATO, the principle is clear separation of levels: strategic, operational and tactical, and maintaining the principle of hierarchicalness in the area of decision-making powers and responsibility of individual command authorities, therefore the Polish Armed Forces system should apply the same solutions. This will ensure efficient and effective cooperation with NATO command structure and allies. The last change in the management and command system, which entered into force in January 2014, basically remodeled the structure and tasks of the leadership and command bodies of the Armed Forces of the Republic. Experiences, analyses and exercises so far point to the shortcomings of the existing system. The following main conclusions and recommendations indicate the need to strengthen strategic competences of the Head of the General Staff of the Polish Army according to the NATO command model. According to the proposed solutions, the head of the General Staff of the Polish Army, being the highest active soldier and candidate for commander-in-chief of the Armed Forces, will have effective instruments to represent the Armed Forces and will be able to agree on all issues relating to the consistency of operational planning in the national and ally dimension on an ongoing basis. At the same time, the Chief of Staff will be the highest soldier in active military service and will lead the entire Armed Forces. Until full capacity is achieved by the Territorial Defence Forces, i.e. until national certification is obtained, the Commander of the Territorial Defence Forces will be directly subject to the Minister of National Defence. After the completion of this process, the Territorial Defence Army, like other types of Polish Armed Forces, will be subordinated to the head of the General Staff of the Polish Army. Such a solution will allow for an undistorted process of forming this extremely important for the security of the state type of Armed Forces. The above solution does not exempt the head of the General Staff of the Polish Army from carrying out planning work related to the use of this type of Armed Forces in the event of a crisis or war. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Generals! Designed law changes will certainly bring a very positive change to the Armed Forces control and command system. They will mean the introduction of a one-man responsibility for commanding the entire Polish Armed Forces, a gradual reform of the management and command system, and thus the possibility of undistorted action in the event of an external threat, and the adjustment of the command system by the Armed Forces to NATO command structures. On behalf of my club, Parliamentary Club Law [[♪ The bell ♪]] and Justice, I declare that we will vote in favour of this law. She's very positive for the security of the state. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u238	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Today we are investigating a government proposal to amend the Act on the Office of the Minister of National Defence and the Act on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland. In a moment, you Members of the Parliament will obediently vote on this bill. But I'm afraid it's going to be reckless, based on the texting instructions. Therefore, on behalf of the Poselski Club, I will briefly tell you about this project. First of all, I will use the Roman couple again: if you want peace, get ready for war. In this spirit, let us look at the justification of the bill. In the introduction, the applicant writes about the particular role played by the presence of allied troops, especially US troops, in Poland, which, in conjunction with the process of strengthening the eastern NATO flank, according to the applicant, created the need to improve the management and command system of the Armed Forces. Do you remember the special role played by the presence of allies, especially Soviet troops, in Poland in 1945 and 1989? At that time, the system of leadership and command of the Armed Forces was so improved that one person autonomously decided about the fundamentals for the security of Poland. This person, like an absolute ruler, was responsible for his decisions only before God. Today, in the justification of the project, we read that the current solution of the command system, e.g. The combination of strategic and operational levels in the national chain of command is not compatible with NATO and most countries of the alliance. For the inconspicuous explanation: now the function of commanders of types of Armed Forces is carried out together by the general commander of types of Armed Forces and the operational commander of types of Armed Forces, while the head of the General Staff of the Polish Army acts as the auxiliary body of the Minister of National Defence during the peace and role of the auxiliary body of the President of the Republic of Poland during the war. The project that is being processed today is overturning this four-year-old order. It extends the role of Chief of the General Staff of the Polish Army to the command authority of the entire Armed Forces. According to the project, the Chief of Staff is directly under the command of the Minister of National Defence, and the Armed Forces is in charge of command of the types of Armed Forces: Commander of the Land Forces, Commander of the Air Force, Commander of the Navy and Commander of the Special Forces. This amendment means that at the head of the Armed Forces, the Chief of Staff under the Minister's authority, and under it there will be four commanders of the Armed Forces who will probably do all the substantive work. Instead of two commanders, the army will be headed by a chief of staff supported by four commanders. Does such a five-man command and control system of the Armed Forces seem to you to be improved? The applicant stresses the need to model our command system on other countries' systems, focusing primarily on the US system. Dear country, the United States has more than 9 million km2 of land, with more than 320 million citizens living there. The United States is the world's military and political superpower, primarily responsible for the safety of our world. The fact that a country like the United States has one and not another command system of an army is due to the peculiarities of that country and its position. But does that mean that we should blindly copy their solutions? Good solutions for Washington need not be good solutions for Warsaw. What else do you want to pick up from our allies and unify? Why don't we put the Polish Army in Bundeswehr uniforms? After all, in Germany they apply and they work. You want peace, get ready for war. How are we supposed to prepare for war when we are discussing laws making further changes to the Armed Forces over and over again? Any change in such a sensitive area as national defence and security should be preceded by analyses, expert debates and should be carried out calmly, without haste. The Sejm should already have complete and complete projects. Instead, we serve the army endless legislative diarrhea. That's the tenth amendment of this law. Tenth since November 2015. Every four months, we shake the Armed Forces. How do you think this translates into Poland's security and trust in our allies? Do you think it is more important to change the chain of command in order to increase our defence capabilities because they are in NATO countries that have it differently than we do? So what? This structure was adopted by Poland four years ago. For four years our allies have known her and worked with her. We did the Anaconda 2016 exercises together. Why change what works? As in the case of junior high schools, introduce a law that will only cause chaos, because of an ideological whim... Let's move on to the last issue, the Territorial Defense Army. The Commission accepted two amendments. It has been stated that the Minister of National Defence directs the Territorial Defence Forces until they are fully able to act, and that this moment is to determine the Minister of National Defence by way of an order. I can't understand the substance of this idea. I have come across the opinion that this is to prevent the introduction of chaos into still organizing territorial defence units, which are supposed to be organized for seven more years. I understand that chaos belongs only to fully organized units of the Armed Forces, but such a translation does not dispel the remaining doubts. How is it that, with the aim of better organisation of the Polish Armed Forces, on the one hand, we want to simplify the decision-making system by transferring full competence to the Chief of General Staff, and on the other hand we do a rip in it leaving the Territorial Defence Army in the hands of the Minister? Such action is an indication that WOT is actually a private minister's army. Why won't you transfer military control to the army? Are you afraid that this is how an Antoni Macierewicz's body will fall out of the closet? As the name suggests, the Territorial Defence Army is a military and should be subordinate to the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland, who have knowledge and skills that allow effective management, not politicians and their political games.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u240	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Public Finance a report on the Government's draft Act on Employee Capital Plans. The Public Finance Commission considered the draft law at its meetings on 25 and 26 September 2018. The idea of employee capital plans is to introduce the first universal, voluntary and private savings system with solidarity between employees, employers and the state. In 10 years, the country will spend more than PLN 30 billion on contributions to the private accounts of Poles in PPK. It is worth noting that during the work of the committee there was an issue which, I think, should also be clarified here in the High House. In the course of the discussion, questions were raised about the privacy of the measures. It is worth to say that the private funds collected in the PPK were guaranteed as such in the draft law. According to Art. 3 PPK law funds collected in PPK constitute the participant's private property, which we can describe as a super-secure investment for the future. We know that the programme, the Act on Employee Capital Plans, is designed to provide additional savings for future pensioners after the age of 60. Then all the funds collected will be available. It is worth pointing out once again that these funds collected in the PPK will be private and inherited funds. As regards the work of the committee, a number of amendments were adopted. Most of these were legislative amendments aimed at clarifying the provisions contained in the original draft, and there were some editorial amendments. It is worth pointing out one of the amendments adopted by the committee - it is about allowing companies in the capital groups to join the PPK within the time limit when the largest company in the group joins the PPK. The Commission also accepted the amendment tabled by the opposition, by the Member of the Modern Club, which aims to extend the catalogue of medical units diagnosed with cancer in the child. This is intended to ensure that the funds paid into the PPK can be used in the event of a serious illness of the child. As regards the key elements of the bill, it is also worth mentioning that there was no objection to the adoption of the article. 134, which clearly indicates the dates of accession of the employment entities to the PPK. From 1 July 2019 entities employing at least 250 people will be able to join the PPK, from 1 January 2020 entities employing at least 50 people. As far as the next deadline is concerned, from 1 July 2020 entities employing at least 20 people will be able to join the PPK. The last date, 1 January 2021, is the date of accession to the PPK of other employment entities and units of the public finance sector. On behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I request that this draft be adopted together with the amendments. Thank you very much. Amendment to Article. 63 refers to the date of storage of the data so far collected in the register - the national system for the information on cosmetics. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u291	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. The first vote will be taken by Mrs Teresa Glenc, Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u292	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the club Law and Justice I have the honour to present the position of the club regarding the government draft Cosmetics Act. The draft Cosmetics Act aims to define the obligations of entities and the competence of bodies in the performance of administrative duties and tasks resulting from the Regulation of the European Parliament of 30 November 2009. The bill aims to ensure a high level of consumer safety and effective supervision of cosmetic products. The project provides the State Sanitary Inspection as well as the State Trade Inspection with new responsibilities related to the supervision of cosmetic products. The draft law adapts the Polish law to the provisions of EU Regulation No 1223/2009 by defining the obligations of manufacturers and distributors of cosmetics, the competence of bodies as regards the correctness of composition, labelling and documentation confirming the safety of products. Manufacturers of cosmetic products will be required to submit an application to the competent district health inspector for inclusion in the list of establishments within 30 days of the start of business. This will enable monitoring of good manufacturing practice. The draft Cosmetics Act proposes the creation of a system solution by establishing a system for informing about serious adverse reactions caused by the use of cosmetic products. The system will collect data on serious adverse reactions caused by the use of cosmetic products. The administrative centre shall be the entity which meets the requirements laid down in the draft law. The bill assumes that data on serious adverse reactions will be reported to the administrative centre. The administrative centre will inform the chief health inspector of the existence of a serious adverse reaction. The Law and Justice Club will vote in favour of the draft Cosmetics Act. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u293	Thank you very much. Mr Rajmund Miller, Civic Platform, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u294	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Speaking on behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I have the honour to present the position of the Platform Club vis-à-vis the government's draft Cosmetics Act. This project is really the implementation of European law on the use of cosmetic products in Poland. It protects the Polish society. The project assumes the creation of a register of companies that produce cosmetic products, or so-called. responsible persons when cosmetic products are not produced in Poland. The project also sets out the obligations of labelling these cosmetic products, i.e. the report on the activities of products in Polish, and the labelling on packaging. The draft shall appoint the authorities responsible for the checks. These authorities are the Chief Sanitary Inspector and Commercial Inspection. The Chief Sanitary Inspector will deal with safety in terms of health impacts on possible consumers, while the Trade Inspection will assess whether the labelling on packaging complies with the provisions of the Act. A very important and important provision of this law is the provision that refers to the establishment of administrative centres to which information on adverse reactions will be transmitted. These centres will decide whether there are serious adverse reactions. Information on these cases will be communicated to the main health inspector of the country and this will be required, in accordance with European law, to provide this information to the European States and to the EFTA associated countries. These are very important information to avoid side effects of these products. It should be noted that all these regulations will be in line with GDPR regulations, with these administrative centres responsible for storing this information. They will also have the power to transmit relevant information on these products to centres treating serious complications after possible use of cosmetic products. Finally, the Act also sets out the financial consequences or penalties to be applied to producers or persons responsible for the distribution of funds related to non-compliance with the provisions of that Law. High Seym! The Civic Platform Club believes that the provisions of this law are desirable, are beneficial for Polish society. The Civic Platform Club will therefore vote in favour of this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u296	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Cosmetic Products Act is intended to regulate this area and to comply with the applicable law, as well as to increase the protection of consumers who have been deprived of such protection so far. Labels and labels on the composition of cosmetics will be harmonised and production control will be tightened. The main objective of this project is to increase consumer safety and to ensure effective supervision of cosmetic products, among others. by compulsory notification of establishments in which cosmetic products are manufactured or packaged, to the list kept by the authorities of the Central Sanitary Inspectorate and to the Trade Inspection. Trade and GIS inspections will receive new tools to increase their supervision of the quality of cosmetic products. The Minister of Health, through a regulation, another legal document issued, will also indicate what test methods will be needed to control the safety of cosmetic products. The new Act will also systematise labelling requirements for cosmetic products. Labels and composition are to be presented only in Polish or English to enable consumers to read them. Manufacturers' declarations will be subject to the control of the GIS and the Trade Inspection. According to the draft law, a system will also be set up to inform about serious adverse reactions caused by the use of cosmetic products. Information on such cases will be included in this register. The new rules are designed to effectively enforce the obligations of cosmetics manufacturers and distributors. Control of cosmetic production will be tightened and, on the other hand, a system of financial penalties imposed by an administrative decision will also be introduced. The project introduces a penalty planner for errors and shortcomings covering both manufacturers and distributors. The bill on cosmetic products has already been positively assessed by the members of the cosmetics manufacturers' associations, among others. by the Polish Association of Cosmetic and Detergent Industry and by the Polish Union of Cosmetic Industry. They found that the new rules would work properly on the Polish market and allow effective supervision and proportionate implementation of the requirements of the EU regulation. The Act is to enter into force on 1 January 2019. The Kukiz Members' Club15 does not see any problem to support this project without any comments. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u298	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the pleasure of presenting my position on behalf of the club Moderna to the government's draft Cosmetics Act. We are in favour of this bill, as are my predecessors. The Act implements the requirements of the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the European Council in Polish legislation and will allow effective supervision of compliance with the provisions of that Regulation. The law is therefore intended primarily to ensure consumer safety. The Cosmetic Products Act is an important act for the Polish economy. Poland is the sixth and will soon be the fifth cosmetic market in the European Union. A significant part of European cosmetics production takes place in Poland. This is a very competitive market. It is estimated that the market for the production of cosmetics in Poland amounts to about PLN 20 billion, with more than 400 companies operating on it. The proposed cosmetic product law will repeal the 2001 Cosmetics Act. The proposed law imposes little obligations on manufacturers and distributors of cosmetics. The law is primarily intended to ensure effective enforcement of the regulations on the Polish market. It shall also lay down financial penalties in the event of any irregularities and shall identify the authorities responsible for their enforcement. The control over the production and marketing of cosmetics will therefore be tightened. The Chief Sanitary Inspector and the State Trade Inspection will receive new tools that will increase the effectiveness of supervision of the quality of cosmetic products. The Act will also systematize requirements related to the labelling of cosmetic products. Descriptions on labels will be drawn up in Polish, with the exception of the composition given by the international nomenclature, in such a way that consumers are properly informed about the properties and use of the product. The Act also clarifies the requirements concerning the language of documentation. The conclusions of the safety assessment of the cosmetic product must be prepared in Polish, while the remaining documentation can be prepared in Polish and English. The possibility of using English will make it easier for manufacturers to create documentation and save a number of translation costs. According to the project, there will also be a System of Information on Serious Unwanted Actions Caused by the Use of Cosmetic Products. That's where we're gonna find information about these cases. The most important new obligation under the Act is to require production facilities to be notified to a centralised database. This is intended to facilitate the supervision of the hygiene conditions of production. In the course of the work, the provisions related to the newly introduced Personal Data Protection Act were also agreed. A wide public consultation was conducted during the drafting of the law. They actively participated in all stages of the industry group. During the work on the Law, a number of changes were made to those provisions which raised opposition by industry representatives, importers, producers, especially small and medium-sized enterprises, as they involved high costs. The most important changes are the reduction of disproportionate administrative penalties, the lack of translation of English-language documentation and the order of reporting of adverse reactions. Compromise solutions were reached, both sides satisfactory. The key demands of the sector in the course of the legislative work have been taken into account. The Act in its current form should function properly on the Polish market. Today, although high penalties raise some concerns for manufacturers and distributors, the industry also has a good assessment of the changes made. The project has a positive opinion of members of industry organizations, in particular the Polish Cosmetics Association and the Polish Cosmetics and Detergent Industry Association. In their view, the new law will allow effective supervision and implementation of the provisions of the EU regulation. In the assessment of the club, the Modern draft Cosmetics Act is a very well prepared legal act. Therefore, the club Modern is in favour of this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u312	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Infrastructure Commission at its meeting on 1 October 2018 examined the Government's draft Print Act No 2808 concerning the amendment of the Act on the Commercialisation of a State-owned public utility enterprise (Polish Post). The purpose of the bill is to enable the sale of independent dwellings at preferential prices to tenants in buildings owned by Poczta Polska. At the moment, in the current legal state, a person who rents an apartment in a residential building belonging to the Polish Post office has the option to buy an apartment with a voucher, provided that the company decides to sell the apartment, while the person who rents an apartment under the same conditions in the non-residential building does not have such possibility. So we're just dealing with legal inequality. And the draft law eliminates this legal inequality. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission recommends that the project be adopted. An amendment to the Polish Post Commercialisation Act has been proposed and this amendment is as if it consumes or eliminates this legal imbalance. The Commission recommends adopting a report in which it is now expressing its support for the project, i.e. to enable tenants to sell their apartments regardless of the function of the building. There were no votes against during the vote. There have been abstentions, but the vast majority recommend adopting this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u313	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Piotr King, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u314	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present our position on the draft law amending the Act on the Commercialisation of a State-owned public utility enterprise - Poczta Polska, sejmowy print nr 2808. High Seym! In the current legal condition, the person who rents an apartment in a residential building belonging to the Polish Post office has the option of buying an apartment with a voucher, provided that the company decides to sell the apartment, while the person who rents an apartment under the same conditions in a non-residential building, even if the building is a redundant company, has not yet had such a possibility. There is therefore a difference in the legal status of tenants depending on whether they rent an apartment in a residential building or in a building with a different function. This must be seen as an inequality between citizens and the law. Therefore, pursuant to this draft law, it is possible to sell self-contained dwellings at preferential prices to tenants in buildings owned by Posta Polska regardless of the function of the building in which these apartments are located. In other words, the project eliminates the problem of the lack of legal solutions enabling Poczta Polska to carry out the above mentioned. legal action. We hope that this bill will be welcomed by those posters whom this provision has so far discriminated against. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this bill. Thank you very much. On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I present an opinion on the draft law amending the Act on the Commercialisation of a State-owned public utility enterprise (Polish Post), print no 2808. So they will be mostly apartments located in buildings where post offices are located. These premises will be sold in accordance with the Law of 15 December 2000 on the rules for the disposal of apartments owned by state-owned enterprises, certain commercial companies with the participation of the State Treasury, state legal persons and certain apartments owned by the State Treasury. This means that eligible persons will be able to acquire such flats with discounts, under the rules laid down in the Real Estate Management Act. The price per apartment may be reduced in total by 6% for each year of work of the entitled person and by 3% for each year of lease of the apartment, with a total reduction not exceeding 95% the sale price of the apartment. Ladies and gentlemen! The Civic Platform in 2015, after the end of its term of office, submitted a bill on the possibility of selling apartments located in buildings owned by Poczta Polska. We appreciate that PiS has taken action launched by the Civic Platform. It is the President and the Management Board of the Company who are responsible for the proper management of the State Treasury's assets, not Members or Senators. Therefore, giving the opportunity to sell these apartments from the resources of the Polish Post, including with a bonus for eligible persons, we call for a thorough and individual examination of each case of the sold dwelling. In this resource because, as you can see in the explanatory memorandum, there are 470 buildings. There are very diverse buildings there. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u334	Good morning. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I understand I'm going to have some time dedicated by your marshal. Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! The questions raised some doubts. These doubts were also expressed in speeches on behalf of the clubs. First of all, it should be emphasized very clearly that thanks to the project, thanks to these provisions, which are included in the draft law, the Management Board of Polish Post will receive an instrument. On the other hand, no specific automaton will be introduced, which will cause the sales process of the apartments to start and be completed. Few of this, the process of selling apartments will start and will involve the need to apply e.g. Ninety-five percent increase. All these possibilities are at the disposal of the board of directors, i.e. the amount of the discount will depend on the price of the square meter of apartments, i.e. up to 90%. The Management Board of the Polish Post will decide, of course, on the basis of estimates, technical descriptions which accompany the valuation of such premises. This Management Board of Polish Post will be able to clearly decide how many of these apartments and which of these apartments will be sold. The provisions of this law give the management the opportunity to increase the efficiency of the company, because unfortunately in the case of Polish Post since 2008, i.e. since the adoption of the Act of 5 September 2008, there has been a provision allowing the sale of apartments regardless of the purpose of the building in which the apartments were sold. This provision was in force for the adoption of these provisions, and later, unfortunately, apartments in non-residential buildings owned by them were excluded. Polish Post Office. On the one hand, housing could be sold in residential buildings, on the other hand, it could not be sold in non-residential buildings. This caused a situation in which even when the Polish Post office was used - and I will remind you that more than 70% Poczta Polska is located in resources, buildings erected before the Second World War - it was not used, it could not be sold without an apartment, and it was not willing to purchase a utility premises with an apartment which was occupied by the tenant. Besides, Poczta Polska could not and did not want to sell apartments with tenants. Therefore, the solution introduced allows the sale of apartments, but it must be made very clear that Poland Post does not enter the housing community. What does that mean? He can't first sell an apartment in a building where he owns a utility establishment. So first, he has to sell the unused, necessary Polish Post office and then sell the apartment. So that it will not be so that Poczta Polska will continue to function so that there is a utility premises not used, the Management Board of Poczta Polska decides on the sale of the apartment, leaves in its resource a utility premises, which is actually not used. We leave these decisions to the Management Board of Polish Post, trusting him and with the conviction that he will legally care primarily about the interests of the organization, the interests of the company. Answers to specific questions. Poczta Polska S.A. has 2 thousand. apartments, exactly 2,000. Apartments, including 598 are inhabited in 460 non-residential buildings, so on the question of how many of these apartments in non-residential buildings will potentially be sold, I answer: 598. But that doesn't mean they're all going to be sold. [[Voice from the room: Of course.]] The assumption is that in the first stage it will be about 75 apartments, because these are the needs arising from the management of Polish Post real estate, resulting from the fact that Poland Post has unfortunately to finance the cost of maintaining these premises. In residential buildings there are 1,400 apartments. In the end, Poczta Polska S.A. intends to leave about 1000 apartments. The sale of these apartments, which the Polish Post Office considers reasonable, will be spread over the years. It's not gonna be a one-time sale, it's not gonna be a one-time hit. As regards the bonus for non-professionals with Polish Post S.A., the law does not differentiate the tenants, i.e. the indicated persons will have the right to the bonus. Employees of Polish Post SA for each year of lease - 3% and for every year of work in Post Office - 6%. The non-workers in Poczta Polska S.A. will receive only a discount for rent for each year - 3%. In addition, I inform you that Poczta Polska SA has not rented apartments for an indefinite period for 10 years. I hope that these answers have satisfied Members who have asked questions. I hope that they resolve the doubts contained in the club speeches. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Voice from the room: 100%.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u343	Thank you very much, sir. Please take the floor of Mr Norbert Kaczmarczyk, Kukiz Club15.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u344	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Poselski Club15 on the report of the committee on the government draft law on the amendment of the Act on countering unfair exploitation of contract advantages in trade in agricultural and food products. The aim of the new normative regulations is primarily to eliminate unfair commercial practices across the entire supply chain of agricultural raw materials and food. The justification for the project indicated the need to include the provisions of the Act in all trade relations between the supplier and the buyer. It is also crucial to ensure the anonymity of the party that makes a notification on practices that unfairly exploit the contractual advantage. The provision on the value of turnover criterion was repealed, which meant that the Act in its previous wording could not have been applied at all. These steps have been taken with a view to significantly extending the number of entities subject to the Act. The existing thresholds were too high and thus narrowed the scope of cases in which the legislation so far applied. The definition of the contract advantage was also greatly simplified, clearly indicating that there was a significant imbalance in the buyer's economic potential vis-à-vis the supplier or supplier vis-à-vis the buyer. Clear and clear. It's a positive change. Polish law must be understood by citizens. A more general definition should also help to eliminate the disadvantage of many agricultural producers vis-à-vis stronger trading partners. Another important change concerns the extension of the circle of entities which will be entitled to notify the suspected use of unfair practices using the contractual advantage. Now it can only be done by an entrepreneur who suspects that such practices are being applied to himself. The changes assume that from now on such a notification will be able to report to anyone. In addition, such a notification will be anonymous as the data identifying the notifier will not be disclosed to the parties to the proceeding at any stage. In conclusion, it should be pointed out that the proposed changes should benefit small enterprises. They should have a positive impact on the competitiveness of the economy, as they will limit the use of unfair commercial practices throughout the food supply chain. I recommend the Kukiz Club to vote 15 for this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Farmers complained about fruit prices in purchases that did not even cover production costs and accused processors of colluding prices. On this project, the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection cooperated with the Ministry of Agriculture. The Office shall monitor the relationship between suppliers and processors. There have been activities that have prevented farmers from planning their deliveries and have not ensured a level playing field between the raw material and all suppliers. There have been cases where payment deadlines have been extended. In the milk processing industry controlled by the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, contracts have sometimes proved to be harmful to farmers who, for example, have been obliged to issue all the milk produced to one processor under threat of penalty. The information identifying the notifier and the content of the notification pursuant to the project will not be disclosed to farmers or traders buying products, which can be used by competition, unless no one has yet said so, and this is an important issue, as part of a market strategy or as part of a competitive struggle. The contractual advantage has been cut to an unprecision significant imbalance in economic potential. This may lead to ambiguities of interpretation during proceedings by the Office, as well as at the stage of negotiation, creation and application of contracts by entrepreneurs. This creates an unclear situation in which entrepreneurs will not know when their activities are limited by the provisions of the Act and when they may involve the imposition of financial penalties. In addition, the revised Article. 28 says: "The President of the Office may give immediate enforcement of the decision in whole or in part if the protection of the essential interests of suppliers or buyers so requires. What exactly are important interests in this case? How do you define that? What's the point of making such imprecise rules? This impreciseness allows a large interference of the office into the free market, because it will depend on the office to define, to interpret unclear rules in a given case. In addition, the revised law will also apply to contracts concluded before its entry into force, which will continue after the amendment. This threatens to sanction contracting parties who may inadvertently violate the newly applicable law. The lack of guidance needed to interpret these provisions may affect the economic decisions of both parties: in the case of buyers, there is a very serious risk of not achieving the objective of this law - the use of intermediaries, the concentration of orders with larger suppliers. The project covers any market relationship concerning the acquisition of agricultural or food products and significantly extends the powers of the President of the Office. The content of the amendment was not consulted publicly at the stage of government work. The project undoubtedly contains some controversy and could have gone to the subcommittee. I think it's a mistake that he didn't make it to this subcommittee, because it is undoubtedly needed, necessary, the farmers were waiting for him, but it contains a lot of problematic records. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u360	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present the position of the Law and Justice Club on the government draft law amending the Animal Products Act and the Food and Nutrition Safety Act, print no. 2858 and 2872. The proposed proposal facilitates food law for operators wishing to produce small-scale food and market it in short distribution chains. To date, operators wishing to carry out an activity in the production of products of animal origin before obtaining an authorisation for the construction of an establishment were required to draw up a technological project for the establishment and send it together with a request for its approval to the district veterinarian competent for its intended place of operation and to notify it in writing of the extent and volume of production and the type of products of animal origin to be produced in that establishment. In addition, in accordance with the current rules, operators wishing to carry out activities on the food market subject to the supervision of the State Sanitary Inspection must, in order to be able to start operations, obtain approval of the establishment. Some of the operators, including those engaged in agricultural retail trade, were exempted from both obligations. According to the draft law, such exemption will also include entities wishing to produce on a small scale in direct sales and marginal, local and limited activities. Those entities will, however, continue to be required to apply for entry in the register of establishments, within at least 30 days before the date of commencement of the planned activity, in writing. The introduction of such facilitation may be an incentive for farmers who intend to produce food and sell it not only to final consumers but also to shops, restaurants or cafeterias within the framework of the abovementioned. forms of activity. For both of these forms sales of food produced from their own raw materials can be made both to the final consumer and to retail establishments directly supplying the final consumer. The facilitations will also apply to the activities of operators wishing to produce small-scale food in rooms mainly used as private dwellings, but where food is regularly prepared to be placed on the market. It concerns food of non-animal origin or food containing food of non-animal origin and products of animal origin at the same time. This facilitates the abolition of the obligation to approve such activities by the competent authority of the State Sanitary Inspection. Operators carrying out such activities will only be required to submit an application for inclusion in the register of establishments. The proposed changes are a way out for small entities intending to produce and sell good, healthy and tasty Polish food. It's a result of consultation and social expectations. It should be added that the proposed changes ensure, which is very important from the consumer's point of view, the safety of food products. It will continue to be necessary to meet hygienic conditions for their production and will be supervised by the Veterinary Inspection - I would remind you that it was intended, among others. additional posts for these purposes - possibly health inspection. For these reasons, the Law and Justice Club supports the proposed bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u364	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Club15 on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Animal Products Act and the Food Safety and Nutrition Act. The main objective of the proposed law is to facilitate food law for those who intend to produce small-scale food and then market it. The proposed normative changes were mainly designed to address the problem of too high food safety requirements for small-scale food-producing operators and market-driven in short supply chains. Currently, entities wishing to carry out activities in the field of production of products of animal origin prior to obtaining a permit for the construction of an establishment are obliged to draw up a technological project of that establishment and send it together with a request for its approval to the district veterinarian. The bill is designed to include, among others, abolition of the obligation to implement and submit to the district veterinarian for approval of the technological project. The obligation to inform the district veterinarian of the extent and volume of production is also abolished. This makes it very easy for small-scale operators, in direct sales and marginal, local and limited activities. In addition, it is proposed to abolish the obligation for district health inspectors or border health inspectors to approve establishments producing and placing on the market food whose production takes place in premises mainly used as private homes, but where food is regularly prepared for placing on the market. These changes should also contribute to reducing regulatory burdens by e.g. shortening the time to settle formalities, reducing the number of procedures or reducing the number of documents required in general. The proposed changes should also contribute to increasing the number of small-scale food production operators and placing them on the market under short supply chains. This should in turn have a positive impact on the availability to consumers of food produced directly by Polish farmers, i.e. food of high quality. In view of the above, Kukiz15 will, of course, vote in favour of this law. Thank you.
