First of all, the bill amending the law on prevention and control of infectious diseases and infections in humans does not introduce anything unusual, nothing that would make us different from other European countries. Does the project aim to eliminate vaccination, as it is used to represent? No. So what is it? It brings the current rules into line with the European rules. Nor is it an anti-implantation project, and above all, it does not in any way cause an epidemic threat. He was born of concern for health and is to restore civil liberties. Why is that? Two types of vaccination are distinguished under the current law: protection in children, those provided for in Article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the current law, and so-called. share, for all, including adults, provided for in Article. Article 33 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the current law. Today both are mandatory in Poland. In 20 European countries, only the latter are mandatory, which are foreseen in the event of an outbreak of infectious diseases. The citizens' bill does not eliminate public vaccinations, nor eliminate preventive vaccinations, but only introduces the voluntaryness of the latter. In the media, where one of the main advertisers is pharmaceutical companies, they say: They want to go back to the Middle Ages. Is that true? Are countries like Austria, Cyprus, Denmark, Estonia, Finland, Germany, Iceland, Ireland, Liechtenstein, Lithuania, Luxembourg, Malta, the Netherlands, Norway, Portugal, Romania, Spain, Sweden, the United Kingdom and Russia in the Middle Ages? Last year, attempts to introduce an obligation to carry out preventive vaccinations among children rejected Denmark, Finland, Sweden and Germany and Norway 3 years earlier. Why? Note: referring to respect for civil rights and the possibility of performing public vaccinations in response to a possible epidemic of this or other infectious disease. Furthermore, Finland was referred to as having no evidence that coercion would in any way promote or maintain a good range of vaccinations, on the contrary - there are studies suggesting that this may have a negative impact. Jonas Sivelä from the Finland Health Institute estimates that coercion can in the long term undermine people's confidence not only in vaccination but also in public health services in general. And this is happening in Poland. Dear Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! Who is aware that the current mandatory vaccination system violates human rights? In the event of complications that the producer himself anticipates, the parent is left to himself, rehabilitates and treats the child, he must resign from his job and, if he wants to claim, he must collect an entry for the court, forensic experts and lawyer without a guarantee of winning, and the producer has unlimited funds. Is that fair? No. The European Court of Human Rights has already challenged such a system of coercion of preventive vaccination in other countries. In the light of these judgments, Poland violates human rights. Art. 8 The Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms states that everyone has the right to respect for their private and family life. The European Court of Human Rights has indicated that compulsory preventive vaccination, the use of which is inevitably linked to the possibility of vaccination complications, including death, is in breach of Article. Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 convention. I'll name it here. judgment of 9 July 2002 Ilaria Salvetti v Italian Government and 15 March 2012 Dmitryevich v Ukraine. In the Italian case Italy was injured, which was affected by paralysis, blindness and speech disturbances as a result of compulsory polio vaccination in 1966 at the age of 5. The case concerned obtaining compensation which was due to a woman due to post-vaccination adverse reactions. The injured party submitted to the Court that the Italian State did not provide adequate compensation for the adverse reactions resulting from compulsory vaccination and therefore infringes the Article. 2 (2) 1, Article Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1, Article 12 and Article 12 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 17 conventions, i.e. the right to life, freedom and personal security, the right to marry and to establish a family, and the prohibition of abuse of rights. Since none of the provisions cited referred to the victim's situation, the Court considered the case on the basis of Article. 8 conventions, i.e. the right to private life, which includes the right to physical and mental integrity. The Court found, inter alia, that compulsory vaccinations as compulsory medical procedures constitute interference with the right to respect for private guaranteed life. I am a doctor with 40 years of practice in the profession, and I want to evaluate the bill from this perspective. In the first sentence of the explanatory memorandum to the citizens' bill amending the Law on the Prevention and Control of Infections and Diseases in People, we can read that it serves to eliminate the preventive vaccination obligation. Based on modern medical knowledge and more than 200 years of research on vaccines, as well as the experience gathered at that time, I am convinced that the purpose of the law indicated by the applicants leads to the complete disqualification of this draft law. Let me also quote a passage from the opinion of the Chief Medical Council. It reads: "The bill must be assessed as dangerous to health safety." Such a clear view indicates the consistent opinion of epidemiology and medicine specialists who have analysed this project. In many studies on vaccination, one can see that they are a victim of their success. As an example, I can give you measles against which mandatory vaccinations were introduced in Poland while I was still in college. In those years, an average of 400 people per 100,000. It was a serious illness. Luckily for us, vaccination was introduced in 1975. Since then, the incidence of measles has gradually decreased with the increase in prognosis, and cases of measles have been individual. It is true that vaccinations do not guarantee 100% effectiveness. None of the doctors will say that each vaccine is safe, but even the person who, despite vaccination, has not acquired sufficient immunity is protected by some kind of protective umbrella, which is spread over it by members of our population in which the vaccine was adopted, thus not giving the virus the possibility to spread. At this point, I would like to address the applicants who are demanding amendments to the bill. Ladies and gentlemen, there are people among us, we often pass them on the street, which for many reasons cannot be vaccinated. They're either cancer patients or those who are before or after transplants. They're fighting for recovery. Exposure to infection by a child who, if lucky, will pass the disease without complications, for many of these people, e.g. receiving immunosuppressants will be a death sentence. A simple coughing in a tram or an elevator meeting can have much more serious consequences for others who are not even vaccinated. The social responsibility to abolish compulsory vaccination would be enormous. Each of those in favour of similar solutions should first of all go to regions where diseases that we consider not to have been eradicated have not been eradicated. Polio or pertussis aren't diseases that can't come back. Therefore, from the beginning of its existence the Modern pays attention to the extremely important role of prevention and postulates in its program, among others. health contributions relief not only for those who are vaccinated on a timely basis with their children, but above all for those who are vaccinated against influenza. Such persons should also be rewarded because they generate much lower costs for the health system. We must appreciate this responsibility. The benefits of vaccination are many times greater than the risks involved. Modern, for which citizens' health is an absolute priority, will vote against this amendment. I would also like to draw attention to one important issue, in my view, concerning the road from production to vaccination. You must know that vaccines require special storage and transport. I myself witnessed in one of the drugstores - I can't say in which city - how flu vaccines have been thawed many times. What is the quality of these vaccines? Of course, it's much worse. In the nearest family, someone vaccinated in the 1960s with Koprowski's vaccine, Salka from Russia did not know it was a vaccine with a poorly attenuated virus. That virus was still dangerous. This man is suffering from severe meningitis and brain disease. Luckily, he came out of it, and he didn't have polio. So let's pay attention. mainly on the way of production, which is well controlled today, but also on the way of distribution, [[♪ The bell ♪]] transport and transfer. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u498	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I will present to you in extenso the position of the government vis-à-vis the citizens' bill amending the law on prevention and control of infectious infections and diseases in humans. If your marshal will allow me, I will answer a few questions as soon as I present my position to the government, and I will also tell you how I feel about it, Jarosław Pinkas. I ask you, the essential intention of the legislative initiative contained in the citizens' draft law amending the Law on the Prevention and Control of Infections and Diseases in Human beings, print No 2796, hereinafter referred to as the draft law, is to abolish the obligation for preventive vaccination referred to in the provisions of the Act of 5 December 2008 on the Prevention and Control of Infections and Diseases in Human Being, hereinafter referred to as the Act, and to introduce the principle of voluntary vaccination. The draft law only leaves the possibility for the Minister responsible for health or voivode to impose an obligation for vaccination in the event of an epidemic or epidemic situation, Article. Point 1 (8). According to Art. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point (1) (a) b and Article. 17 There is a legal obligation to undergo protective vaccination against certain infectious diseases. Subjective scope of the above mentioned application. obligation, i.e. groups of persons obliged to undergo protective vaccination, and scope, i.e. the infectious diseases covered by the preventive vaccination obligation are laid down in the Regulation of the Minister for Health of 18 August 2011 on compulsory preventive vaccination. On the other hand, specific indications for individual vaccines resulting from the current epidemiological situation are set out in the preventive vaccination programme for a given year published in the form of a communication from the Chief Health Officer. It should be noted that the obligation for designated groups of persons to undergo protective vaccination against certain infectious diseases has existed in the Republic of Poland for nearly 60 years. It is an administrative obligation of a non-monetary nature arising directly from the provisions of the Act. Persons obliged to exercise their legal obligation to undergo vaccination of children or persons not capable of legal action shall be those exercising legal custody or guardianship within the meaning of Article. 3 (1) (a) 1 point 1 of the Act of 6 November 2008 on the rights of the patient and the spokesperson on the rights of the patient. The implementation of the preventive vaccination programme plays an important role in shaping public health in the Republic of Poland. In the area of prevention and prevention of infectious diseases, this implementation also takes into account the international obligations of the Republic of Poland to combat and eradicate communicable diseases by means of preventive vaccination. It depends on the level of public acceptance, the legal obligation to undergo protective vaccinations, the integrity of the activities of doctors, nurses, midwives carrying out compulsory vaccinations and the activities of administrative authorities to enforce the obligation of vaccination by persons legally obliged to do so. Compulsory protective vaccinations are an effective and generally socially acceptable method to prevent infectious diseases, both in terms of prevention of these diseases in a person undergoing protective vaccination, individual prevention, and in terms of shaping the resistance of the entire population to diseases, so-called. collective prevention. A high proportion of vaccinated individuals are required to achieve immunity on a population scale. Typically, population immunity is achieved with vaccination of a high percentage of the population, i.e. at least 95%, which effectively prevents the spread of infectious diseases not only in immunized individuals, but also in those who, due to health contraindications, cannot be vaccinated against these diseases, or who have not developed immune immunity despite proper vaccination. In the past, the prevalence of preventive vaccination has enabled the eradication of smallpox. At present, worldwide eradication programmes such as polio and measles are being carried out together with the World Health Organisation. The introduction of compulsory preventive vaccination against selected infectious diseases also reduces the social consequences associated with the severe health consequences of infectious diseases, including the burden on the public finance sector of the costs of treating and treating the complications of these diseases. The introduction of universal vaccination in the most effective manner has resulted in the eradication of the risk of previously common infectious diseases, in particular in the paediatric population. The provision of a high proportion of people immune to the population shall be supported by educational and health measures promoting knowledge of the role of vaccination in the prevention of infectious diseases, but at the same time protected by legal administrative enforcement measures in relation to the implementation of this vaccination obligation by persons obliged to do so. It is now difficult to predict what the consequences of the abolition of the preventive vaccination obligation provided for in the draft law will be for the country's immunization. It should be borne in mind that, once the preventive vaccination obligation has been lifted, further yearbooks of children and adolescents will only be vaccinated at a rate of not more than 70%. , which in a few years will create conditions for the epidemic spread of infectious diseases in the population of children and young people, especially such highly contagious diseases as measles, mumps or rubella, and will allow the occurrence of infectious diseases that are extremely dangerous, currently almost absent in Poland, such as. tetanus, diphtheria or polio. However, the effects of the abolition of the vaccination obligation on the immunization of the next year of children and adolescents and the entire population are difficult to predict, as no country with similar socio-economic conditions and legal arrangements for preventive vaccination has abolished the vaccination obligation, especially in the period of increased activity of anti-vaccine movements, which already test not only the preventive vaccination obligation itself, but also the appropriateness of carrying out preventive vaccinations for the benefit of children, effectively for the prevention of infectious diseases. Thus, regression in the fight against infectious diseases through preventive vaccination can significantly reduce the credibility of the Republic of Poland towards partners carrying out WHO coordinated international programmes for the eradication of infectious diseases. Experience in some countries, e.g. Italy, France and Germany, which previously, under different legal, socio-economic and cultural conditions in these countries, did not have an obligation to immunize or abolish it, and are now introducing elements of the vaccination obligation for children due to the growing maturity of infectious diseases in these countries, should lead to careful withdrawal from the vaccination obligation in Poland. Notwithstanding all the arguments previously mentioned for maintaining the mandatory nature of preventive vaccination against selected infectious diseases, the proposed law contains a number of errors. Projectors are inconsistent despite the abolition of the vaccination obligation in Article. Point 1 (3) of the draft law regulates the activities related to the carrying out of preventive vaccinations previously compulsory, within their meaning being a different type of vaccination than the recommended protective vaccinations, and in Article. Point 1 (6) of the draft law, despite the abolition of the vaccination obligation, add a new provision, Article. 21a Act, which provides that provisions do not restrict the possibility of voluntary vaccination. If the vaccination obligation is lifted, the distinction between these two categories of vaccination is incomprehensible and unnecessary. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Point 1 (5) of the draft law provides that the inclusion in the register of adverse vaccination reactions reported by a doctor of an adverse vaccination reaction, NOP, will require the issuing of an administrative decision by the county health inspector. It is not specified whether the administrative procedure for the decision to enter the NOP in the register will be carried out ex officio or at the request of the party, or whether the NOP recognition physician reporting the NOP is also a party to the proceedings. The proposed amendment shall be introduced in place of the substantive decision of the district health inspector on the entry of the notified NOP case in the register where it meets the NOP criteria set out for epidemiological surveillance of the NOP from the Health Minister's Regulation of 21 December 2010 on undesirable vaccination reactions and their recognition criteria. In addition, in Article 2, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 1 point (1) (a) and the draft law proposes that the preventive vaccination programme for a given year, containing the substantive guidelines for doctors and nurses implementing preventive vaccinations concerning the use of individual vaccines, be published annually by means of a regulation of the Minister responsible for Health, and not, as yet, by means of a communication from the Chief Health Inspector. Such a solution is not appropriate due to the subject matter of regulation, which is strictly substantive, resulting from the changing epidemiological situation and the progress of vaccinology. At the same time, it should be noted that the proposed law does not provide for transitional provisions allowing addressees to adapt to changes in the legal situation or the corresponding vacatio legis. In the light of the above, the Council of Ministers has a negative opinion on the citizens' bill amending the law on the prevention and control of infectious infections and diseases in humans, print No 2796. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u507	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed law amends the Maritime Fisheries Act of 19 December 2014 including: the creation of a new central government body, i.e. the chief maritime fisheries inspector, the creation of an opportunity to exchange individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels and to adapt the legislation on the register of fishing vessels and Union regulations. The most important element of the proposal to amend the Maritime Fisheries Act is to harmonise the way in which commercial and recreational fisheries operators are inspected by establishing a single central government body established to control compliance with the rules on marine fisheries and the organisation of the fish market, i.e. Chief Inspector of Maritime Fisheries, based in Słupsk, in place of three previously functioning Regional Inspectors of Maritime Fisheries, based in Gdynia, Słupsk and Szczecin. The current division into three regional maritime fisheries inspectors in Gdynia, Slupsk and Szczecin does not ensure uniform supervision of compliance with the rules on marine fisheries and the organisation of the fish market. The new organisational structure will ensure that one central state administration takes over the competence to control compliance with the rules on sea fishing and the rules on the organisation of the fish market. The target organisational model for fisheries control services is to set up a central government body appointed by the President of the Council of Ministers at the request of the Minister responsible for fisheries and directly subordinate to that Minister. This body will be responsible for the tasks related to the control of compliance with the rules on marine fisheries and on the organisation of the fish market, as well as coordination of inspection and inspection activities of Poland in the framework of the achievement of the objectives of the Common Fisheries Policy. The location of the new body in Słupsk will allow the use of properties used by the Regional Fisheries Inspector in Słupsk. At the same time, the city of Słupsk is located, as you know, in the central part of Pomerania, which will allow for the smooth movement of the chief inspector and subordinate units between ports and seaports, and for efficient and direct supervision of fisheries inspectors carrying out their tasks in coastal towns, which is now a big problem. The proposed law assumes that the territorial scope of the main inspector will cover the territory and the exclusive economic zone of the Republic of Poland. In the territory of the exclusive zone of the Republic of Poland, i.e. the Baltic Sea area adjacent to the territorial waters of our country, fishing is carried out over which the control is to be exercised, among others. a new body. It is therefore necessary to define the territorial scope of the operation of the inspector, in particular as an area of its operation, in order not to restrict the control by the Polish inspection of fisheries exclusively to the territory of the Republic of Poland, i.e. to an area of 12 nautical miles, which is, of course, caused by the robbery policy of our neighbours when it comes to resources in the Baltic Sea. In order to carry out the tasks efficiently and effectively, the inspector may, but no longer need to set up out-of-town centres. And most importantly, ladies and gentlemen, the solutions adopted meet the expectations of the fishing environment, assume that individual fishing quotas can be replaced in whole or in part between shipowners of fishing vessels allocated to the area by the individual fishing quotas of the species of marine organisms concerned by this exchange. Exchanges may be carried out in accordance with the conversion factor for the quantity of marine organisms of the species concerned subject to an individual fishing quota which may be indicated for the quantity determined by the quota in question. The specific conversion factor shall be determined annually by the Minister responsible for fisheries by means of a regulation on the basis of the overall catch quotas and the market value of the various species of marine organisms. The Minister shall also lay down detailed conditions for the exchange of individual fishing quotas. I ask you to support our project. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u508	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Dorota Arciszewska-Mielewczyk will be the first on behalf of the Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u509	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed Law amending the Maritime Fisheries Act introduces a number of amendments to the existing legislation and their overarching objective is to create a chief maritime fisheries inspector as a central government body. The draft law also creates opportunities for the exchange of individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels and adapts the provisions of the Act concerning the register of fishing vessels to Union regulations. The creation of a single central government body set up to control compliance with the rules on marine fisheries and the organisation of the fish market - the chief maritime fisheries inspector based in Słupsk, in place of the three previously functioning regional maritime fisheries inspectors, is intended to lead to a uniform way of carrying out inspections of operators engaged in commercial and recreational fishing. The division into three regional maritime fisheries inspectors in Gdynia, Słupsk and Szczecin, which is currently in operation, does not ensure uniform supervision of compliance and control and thus creates a different approach to the way in which the legal actions are carried out. The changes will aim to standardise and harmonise control activities, use of existing staff resources, use of existing regional locations of marine fisheries inspectors. The final organisational model of maritime fisheries control services provides for the establishment of a central government body appointed by the President of the Council of Ministers on a proposal from the Minister responsible for fisheries and directly subordinate to the Minister for Maritime Fisheries. The Chief Inspector of Maritime Fisheries will be responsible for the tasks related to the control of compliance with the rules on marine fisheries and the organisation of the fish market, as well as coordination of inspection and inspection activities of Poland in the framework of the achievement of the objectives of the Common Fisheries Policy. The Deputy Chief Inspector for Maritime Fisheries shall be appointed at the request of the Chief Inspector for Maritime Fisheries by the Minister responsible for Fisheries. The statutes of the Chief Inspector for Maritime Fisheries shall be issued in the form of a decree by the Minister responsible for Fisheries. The rules of organisation of the Chief Maritime Fisheries Inspector shall be drawn up in accordance with the provisions of the Statute and approved by the Minister responsible for Fisheries. The head inspector will be Słupsk. The selection of Słupsk as the seat of the new body will allow the use of buildings which are currently serving as the seat of the Regional Maritime Fisheries Inspectorate in Słupsk, and Słupsk is located in the central part of the Polish Pomerania and will allow for the smooth movement of the chief maritime fisheries inspector between ports and maritime marinas and efficient and direct supervision of marine fisheries inspectors who perform their duties outside Słupsk. The Chief Inspector of Maritime Fisheries will carry out inspection and inspection activities and will conduct: accounting, personnel and public procurement wages, planning and management of quality control, internal audit and defence and protection of classified information. The territorial scope of the main inspector for sea fishing covers the territory and the exclusive economic zone of the Republic of Poland. The draft law provides that for the efficient and effective performance of the tasks of the Chief Inspector, local centres may be set up to operate the Chief Maritime Inspector in their area of activity, to be specified in the order of the Minister responsible for fisheries. The solutions presented in the proposed law are intended to allow continuity of administrative cases and proceedings to date. Regional marine fisheries inspectors and their alternates will receive a proposal for employment in the newly created structure in accordance with the proposed law. In addition to the creation of a new administration, the draft law creates the possibility to exchange individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels in accordance with the principle of species per species. Regulation is intended to allow for a more efficient use of fishing quotas while there is no undesirable phenomenon of trade in fishing quotas. The solution meets the expectations of the fishing environment. Exchanges may be carried out in accordance with the conversion factor for the quantity of marine organisms of the species concerned subject to an individual fishing quota which may be indicated for a specific quantity of marine organisms of another species covered by an individual fishing quota in the area concerned. A detailed conversion factor shall be determined by the Minister responsible for fisheries by means of a regulation. The Minister responsible for Fisheries shall also specify the detailed manner in which the exchange of individual fishing quotas is to be carried out. The draft law also provides for amendments to the rules on fishing vessel registers, which include: they adapt them to the relevant EU legislation and are of a sequencing nature. The draft law provides that its provisions should enter into force on 1 January 2019, with the exception of the provisions relating to the representative who will organise the new office and the amendments which adapt the national provisions relating to the fishing vessel register to the Union. Law and Justice Club [[♪ The bell ♪]] requests that the project be forwarded to the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u510	Thank you, Member. Mr Jerzy Borowczak, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u511	Thank you, Marshal. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the government draft law amending the Maritime Fisheries Act, print No 2810. Maritime fisheries are not political, especially the current one. Therefore, I ask that our comments and doubts should not be read as a political dispute. Our intention is to understand the organisational changes that have been made in fisheries surveillance. It is hard to get the impression that even in such an apolitical area as sea fishing, the proposed amendments to the amendment of the Law on Maritime Fisheries are fully in line with the policy of the current governing team, whose aim is to centralise the state. How can I read and understand the creation of a new central government body in the form of a major maritime fisheries inspector? The current structure of district inspectors in Szczecin, Słupsk and Gdynia meets the requirements, and the maritime fisheries administration is the minister responsible for fisheries and the district inspectors under the authority of the Minister. It is difficult to see in the proposed new central state administration a qualitative change improving supervision of compliance and organisation of the fish market. The new central state body, apart from additional costs and additional posts, centralises the entire supervisory structure, and every form of centralism leads to ossification of structure, perversions and reduced efficiency and quality of operation. Therefore, we would like to hear from the applicant what tangible savings this will bring to the new central government body and what benefits it will bring in quality, in monitoring compliance with regulations and the organisation of the fish market in Poland. Unfortunately, the provisions of the amendment of the law and its justification do not give rise to these benefits. Only the pursuit of democratic centralism can be seen as consistent with the rest of the policies pursued. But it's not all our doubts. The amended law was consulted extensively, as many as 60 social and professional organisations. It's just that there's not much to this consultation. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u519	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Parliamentary Club15, I have the honour to present my position on the government proposal for a law amending the Maritime Fisheries Act, print No 2810. The proposed amendments harmonise the way in which commercial and recreational fisheries operators are inspected by establishing a single body appointed to control compliance with the rules on marine fisheries and the organisation of the fish market. The bill provides for the appointment of the Chief Inspector of Maritime Fisheries based in Słupsk. Currently, there is a division between three regional marine fisheries inspectors: Szczecin, Słupsk and Gdynia. We agree with the applicants that such a division is unfavourable. Currently 47 inspectors are employed, who are subject to the three Regional Maritime Fisheries Inspectors. Each district inspector has internal procedures that are incomplete and often outdated, which makes checks inconsistent. The organisational structure of the office serving the Chief Inspector will be established on the basis of existing staff and consequently employment will remain at the same level. The aim is also to use existing locations in which district inspectors are currently located, which will not result in additional costs from the state budget. The only expenditure foreseen involves technical changes such as the exchange of information boards, business cards or badges, which is set at 60,000. zł. The draft also provides for an extension of the list of documents attached to the application for registration of the vessel in the fishing vessel register or to an application for amendment of the entry in the register. These changes are due to the provisions of the Maritime Chamber on registered matters, which are often issued much earlier than the ship's certificate, i.e. they are more convenient documents for the owner of the fishing vessel, enabling him to obtain prior registration of the vessel in the fishing vessel register or to change the entry. In addition, the Act introduces provisions allowing the exchange of individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels, in accordance with the principle of species per species. Consequently, this will allow more efficient use of fishing quotas and thus reduce the risk of trade in such quotas. Following consultation with the environment concerned and a positive response to the proposed solutions, the Kukiz Club supports this draft law.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u520	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! This bill assumes, among others, the establishment of a major maritime fisheries inspector based in Słupsk. A new central government body will be set up in place of current regional inspectors of marine fisheries in Szczecin, Słupsk and Gdynia. The Chief Inspector for Maritime Fisheries will be appointed by the Prime Minister at the request of the Minister for Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation. How will this change affect the status of employment in the Maritime Fisheries Inspectorate? Will there be an increase in employment in the above mentioned period. institutions? Will the employees of the existing district inspectors get a guarantee of employment after the reorganisation of the institution? The project under investigation also introduces provisions for the exchange of individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels, in accordance with the principle of species per species. Will the proposed wording not result in an undesirable phenomenon of trafficking in such amounts?
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u521	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! The proposed law amends the Maritime Fisheries Act of 19 December 2014 (Journal of Laws No. U. z 2018 poz. 514 and 650) in order to harmonise the way in which commercial and recreational fisheries operators are inspected by setting up a single body appointed to control compliance with the rules on marine fisheries and the organisation of the fish market. Słupsk will be the seat of the Chief Inspector of Maritime Fisheries. At the same time, the possibility for the Minister responsible for fisheries to set up and abolish the local centres of that body by means of an order. The proposed law also creates, by adding to the provisions of the Maritime Fisheries Act of 19 December 2014 a proposed article. 53a, the possibility to exchange individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels, in accordance with the principle of species per species. It assumes that individual fishing quotas may be exchanged in whole or in part between shipowners of fishing vessels allocated to the area concerned by the individual fishing quotas of the species of marine organisms concerned. Exchanges may be carried out in accordance with the conversion factor for the quantity of marine organisms of the species concerned subject to an individual fishing quota which may be indicated for a specific quantity of marine organisms of another species covered by an individual fishing quota in the area concerned. A detailed conversion factor shall be determined annually by the Minister responsible for fisheries, by means of a regulation, on the basis of the overall catch quotas and the market value for each species of marine organisms and in accordance with the provisions of the Act. The Minister shall also lay down detailed conditions for the exchange of individual fishing quotas. Thus, the Minister of PiS will determine and manually control the exchange of individual fishing quotas between shipowners of fishing vessels. The planning economy is coming to us with great steps. The Modern Posel Club will vote in favour of referring the bill to further work on the committee. Thank you very much.

I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice of Mr Łukasz Piebiak to present the justification for the bill. Hello, Minister. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u524	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the government bill on the European political party and European political foundation, Parliamentary printing no. 2827. The aim of the proposed law is to ensure effective application in Poland of Parliament and Council Regulation No 1141/2014 on the status and financing of European political parties and European political foundations. This regulation regulates the legal status of European political parties and related European political foundations as bodies pursuing European political objectives. The proposed law is intended to lead to the adoption of solutions concerning those entities with the seat of the Republic of Poland concerning matters which have not been sufficiently regulated in Regulation No 1141/2014. The proposal therefore contains technical solutions that clarify the provisions of the Regulation and enable it to be applied. The Regulation grants both parties and foundations a European legal personality which is linked to the registration in the register of European political parties and European political foundations operated by the Office for Foreign Affairs. European political parties and European political foundations operating in the European Parliament. The proposed solutions will include in particular a closer definition of the national legal regime for such entities established in Poland. As a general rule, the rules on political parties will apply to the European political party established in the Republic of Poland and to the EPP, respectively, to the association. The proposed law will also regulate the national aspects of the formation and liquidation of EPP and EFP established in Poland, in particular the tasks of the registry court and the court keeping the political party records, as well as supervision and cooperation measures with the Office for Foreign Affairs. European political parties and European political foundations. The proposed law provides that the tasks of the Polish courts will relate in particular to the issue of certificates stating that the applicant requesting the registration of an EPP or an EFP based in the Republic of Poland fulfils all the relevant national requirements and its statutes comply with the law. The tasks of the Polish courts will also include activities related to the deletion of the legal predecessor of the European political foundation, i.e. associations, from the National Court Register, provided that the creation of a European political foundation takes place by means of a transformation from the association, and the liquidation of EPP and EFP established in Poland on a similar basis to those applied to national political parties and associations. The draft law provides that the national contact point for the exchange of information on the EPP and the EFP with the Office for Foreign Affairs. The European political parties and European political foundations will be the State Election Commission. In addition, as regards the supervision of the functioning of the EPP and the EFP, the proposed law entrusts the prosecutor-general with competence to act on behalf of the Republic of Poland to the Office for Foreign Affairs. European political parties and European political foundations with a reasoned request to remove a European political party or European political foundation from the register kept by the office in the event of a serious breach of national law and after the measures provided for in national law have been exhausted. It should be noted that there are currently no European political parties and European political foundations based in Poland. Most of the more than a dozen such entities are based in Brussels. However, it is the responsibility of the Polish legislator to create a legal framework that will enable such entities to operate in Poland, which is the purpose of this submission. I think that information is sufficient. The project aims to implement the obligations arising from membership of the European Union and does not appear to be particularly controversial. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u525	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. This possibility is important from the point of view of the regulation on the legal status of these entities as pursuing policy objectives at European level. The Regulation grants the EPP and the EFP European legal personality, regulates the emergence of these entities, their internal organisation, conditions and registration procedures, as well as the deletion from the register and the rules for financing and reporting of these entities and for controls and penalties imposed by the European Parliament's office. The right assumption of the draft law is the introduction of a precise national legal framework which will apply to those entities established in Poland. The wording used in the draft for the application to the European political party of the political party is rightly not limited to the provisions of the Political Party Act, but also to other legal acts in so far as they are applicable to the rights and obligations of political parties. Members of such European parties may also be individuals, but the principle is that membership of a European political party is intended primarily for an existing, functioning party in a Member State. Any such or similar powers shall remain within the competence of the Member States. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u540	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The Act is another legal act that we would like to introduce solutions that will implement the philosophy of our government - making Polish law, Polish laws, Polish legal system as friendly as possible to entrepreneurs. We are talking about aspects such as mutual recognition of benefits, e-protocol from technical supervision activities carried out, faster flow of information about damage to the device. Today, when we use the word "easy" in this law, it is certainly important to stress the solution which seems obvious and has not yet been implemented, namely mutual recognition of certificates issued by individual authorities. We are talking about the Technical Control Office, the Transport Dosor Technical and Military Technical Dosor. Finally, entrepreneurs will save time and money because the certificates will be mutually recognised by these offices. It will also be safer because our goal is to increase the safety of the operation of technical equipment. For this purpose, the validity periods of the attestations have been set. We are talking about a minimum period of 5 years or a maximum period of 10 years. The period of validity of these certificates will depend on the type of device. At present such certificates are issued indefinitely, but, of course, the usefulness of the equipment is not unlimited but limited, and it is necessary that the supervisory authorities be able to verify their safety and suitability for use during certain periods. However, at the request of the trader himself, the validity of the certificate may be extended free of charge. It'll be faster as well. It will also introduce the possibility of drawing up protocols from the technical supervision activities carried out in electronic form, which will facilitate and speed up cooperation with entrepreneurs. This will also reduce costs in the company and improve the circulation of documents. I would also like to emphasise the issue of the exchange of information. Analysis by the Technical Inspection Office and the State Labour Inspection reports show that the cause is more than 90% Unhappy accidents and dangerous damage to the operation and operation of technical equipment were errors in the use of them. As a result of the proposed changes, the maintenance of the technical equipment will be required to notify the relevant technical oversight unit as soon as possible of any dangerous damage or accident related to the use of the device. At the same time, public administrations will provide this information to the technical oversight unit. On the other hand, at the request of public administrations, the Technical Supervision Office will make available information from the records of the technical equipment used. In a word, it is about the efficient flow of information, so that everything is transparent and can serve entrepreneurs - especially entrepreneurs - but also public administrations in carrying out their basic tasks. We are also strengthening ministerial oversight of the bodies of technical oversight units. The new rules provide for the strengthening of the supervision of the Minister responsible for Economic Affairs of the Technical Supervision Authority, similarly as regards the Minister responsible for Transport when we are talking about the Transport Technical Dosor. The Minister will be entitled, among other things, to this task. the right to approve the accounts of the Offices' activities. In conclusion, Mr. Marshal, High Izbo, it's supposed to be easier, faster, cheaper, safer, while the surveillance offices will become more business friendly, because they're supposed to be partners in carrying out tasks, earning money, not getting mixed up with the controls and burdens they impose. We want to carry out reforms that will promote entrepreneurship and promote the creation of a friendly, effective and partnership image towards the entrepreneur and the citizen of the administration. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u542	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It has been my honor to present my position on the draft law amending the Law on Technical Dosor, print no. 2826. The draft concerns the alignment of the provisions of the Technical Dosorage Act with the current legal situation in other areas affecting the operation of the relevant technical supervision units and the improvement of safety in the operation of technical equipment subject to technical supervision. The proposed amendments to the Law of 21 December 2000 on Technical Dosore, hereinafter referred to as the Technical Dosore Act, are in line with the implementation of the Responsible Development Strategy adopted by the Council of Ministers. The work undertaken in 2017 on the revision of this law aims at adapting the provisions of the Act to the current legal situation in other areas affecting the operation of the relevant technical supervision units and increasing the safety in the operation of technical equipment subject to technical supervision. These amendments aim to clarify the rules and improve the selected areas of the technical supervision system while reducing administrative barriers for entrepreneurs. The proposed provisions also implement the recommendations made following the inspection of the Supreme Audit Chamber at the Technical Supervision Office in 2016. Technical oversight bodies shall carry out statutory tasks to ensure the safe operation of technical equipment. The activities in this area have a direct bearing on the safety of operators operating technical equipment, operating, maintaining, repairing, modernising, but above all bystanders often unaware of the existence of operating technical equipment in their surroundings. The tasks carried out therefore fall within the general concept of public security. In the current legal state, the minutes of the technical supervision unit's performance shall be drawn up only in paper form. Although the provisions of the Act do not impose a specific form of protocols, written or electronic, it is appropriate to specify the possibility to draw up such protocols also in electronic form. In addition, the existing provisions of the Technical Dosore Act use terminology for determining the level of fees for checking qualifications which have not been applied since 2003. In this respect, it is necessary to adapt the rules to the existing legal situation. Verification of the qualifications of persons operating and maintaining technical equipment shall be confirmed by certificates of qualification issued directly. Studies and analyses of accidents on equipment subject to technical supervision show that operational errors are the main cause of them. According to the Office of Technical Supervision, the analysis of accidents and hazardous damage to technical equipment related to 2016, as many as 94.5% the causes of unfortunate accidents are operational errors, including those related to the operation and maintenance of technical equipment. In addition, an important proposal for a change is to introduce in the Act solutions enabling the use of one document - decision, qualification certificate - in Poland empowering entities to manufacture, repair or modernise (a right in the form of a decision) and among others. operation and maintenance. This is a qualification certificate for technical equipment for which both WDT, TDT and UDT can be appropriate, i.e. voivodship technical supervision, Technical Transport supervision and Technical Supervision Office. At present, doubts in such cases may arise from the lack of sufficient precision of the law as to the authority's competence, in particular in the event of a partial overlap of such powers. This law introduces the possibility for these three supervisory bodies to honour decisions and certificates issued. High Izbo! The Act does not provide for financial implications for both the state budget and companies, and significantly increases the level of security, simplifies procedures for both offices and companies. This is a very necessary law and enters into force on 1 January 2019. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The Law and Justice Club will vote in favour and I ask the High House to approve this law. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u560	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present to the High House a draft law amending the Police Act and some other laws. The provisions of the proposed law are to be a continuation of the changes to the Polish anti-terrorist system, which was initiated by the Sejm with the adoption of the Act of 10 June 2016 on anti-terrorist activities. The solutions introduced at the time were the first stage of changes in the Polish anti-terrorist system and were a response to an increase in the terrorist threat in Europe. They were also an expression and manifestation of the implementation of the Law and Justice Programme, because we announced such actions and such a need. We have identified this need by preparing our programme, even like other European countries that have already had such rules in their systems. These solutions were also involved in international action to strengthen the tools to counter and respond to terrorist incidents. From the point of view of the coordination of national services and bodies forming part of the anti-terrorism system, the most important solution contained in the Act was the introduction of a precise division of tasks carried out in the various phases of anti-terrorist action. In accordance with the rules, the Head of the Internal Security Agency is responsible for preventing terrorist incidents and for preparing to take control of terrorist incidents, reacting in the event of such incidents and restoring the resources to respond to those events, the Minister responsible for Home Affairs. The consequence of this division of competences was the introduction of a detailed mechanism for the coordination of terrorist incidents at the site. The Act identifies the Police as the formation responsible for directing counter-terrorism activities. These include counter-terrorist actions, i.e. actions against perpetrators of terrorist offences, carried out using specialised forces and means and specialised tactics to eliminate the direct threat of life, health or freedom of persons or property. This issue is directly reflected in the catalogue of statutory tasks set out in the Police Act. In accordance with the Anti-Terrorism Act, a police officer designated to direct anti-terrorist activities has been assigned specific powers, inter alia. with regard to the management of the counter-terrorist group. The group may include police officers, Border Guards, Internal Security Agency and soldiers of the Military Gendarmerie or the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. Importantly, the group was granted the possibility of the so-called. the special use of weapons in cases where this is necessary to counter an attack on human life or health or to release a hostage, and the use of firearms in a manner that does the least possible harm is insufficient and it is not possible to counter such an attack or release of the hostage otherwise. Dear Members, High Seym! The changes introduced by the Anti-Terrorism Act have been developed at the level of internal police regulations, which was the second stage of changes in the Polish anti-terrorist system. In order to improve the structure of counter-terrorist units of the Police by decision of the Chief of Police of 21 April 2017, the anti-terrorist sections of the police's own anti-terrorist units have been replaced by the anti-Terrorist units of the Police which functioned so far. This has brought together the structure of anti-terrorist units at the level of voivodship commands. However, these changes require a continuation at the statutory level. The police's own anti-terrorist units, as well as the anti-terrorist sections, were treated for years mainly as units to support criminal and investigative services, among others. during arrests of particularly dangerous perpetrators. In order to ensure the conditions for a more effective implementation of the action, it is necessary to define more effective mechanisms for the disposal of counter-terrorism units and counter-terrorism units throughout the country and the introduction of a uniform chain of command. It is also necessary to harmonise the tactics of action and the level of training of these units. To this end, the proposed law amending the Police Act and certain other laws provides for separation within the framework of the Police Counter-Terrorism Service. This service will be entrusted with the responsibility for counter-terrorist activities and supporting the activities of police agencies under conditions of special threat or requiring the use and application of specialised forces and means and specialised tactics of action. This will increase the capacity to respond to terrorist threats as well as improve the activities of other police units related to the implementation of particularly dangerous activities. The service will consist of the Central Counter-Terrorist Division of the BOA Police and independent units of counter-Terrorist Police. In the name of the Central Counter-Terrorist Division of the BOA Police, a shortcut to the name of the existing Anti-Terror Operations Office was left in the bill. This is due to the recognition of the above. the names in the Europol forum of the Atlas Group, which brings together the special intervention units of the European Union States. Besides, this name is already in the Polish legal system, in naming, in associations strongly established, so we leave it. The BOA will be an organizational unit of the Police directly subordinate to the Chief Police Officer. Supporting activities of the BOA in the field of organizational, personnel, logistics and technical services will be provided by the Police Headquarters. High Seym! In order to harmonise the system of operation and the organisation of independent police counter-terrorist divisions, the proposed law provides for the BOA to assign competence to coordinate, prepare and implement counter-terrorist actions of the police, including to direct to the activities of independent police counter-terrorist units. Thus, the BOA will exercise substantive supervision over the implementation of combat and training activities and the use of independent forces and resources of the police counter-terrorist divisions. This element was missing in the current legal situation. The project also foresees that counter-terrorism activities will be carried out by the BOA and the police's own counter-terrorism units with priority before other actions, which will provide adequate tools to respond to terrorist incidents. The key elements to ensure the effectiveness of counter-terrorism activities are the speed of response and the ability to provide adequate police forces with a uniform chain of command. An important element of the proposed solutions is the provision of appropriate qualifications for counter-terrorism command staff. The project adopted a solution whereby the commander of the BOA and his deputy, as well as the commanders and deputy commanders of the counter-terrorist units of the police, will be appointed from among the officers of the counter-terrorist service. The command of counter-terrorism activities requires special knowledge and experience, specific qualifications. They can only be acquired when serving in the counter-terrorist unit, including the Police Division, which will be under the command of the commanders or deputy commanders of these structures, and thus involve the execution of activities using specialized tactics and in life-threatening conditions. The project also provides for candidates to be appointed as commander of the BOA, its alternates, as well as commanders of independent units of counter-terrorist police and their alternates to verify the conduct of the service or to guarantee the proper performance of their duties. These commanders will be included in the list of posts which are already subject to such a verification procedure. High Seym! Specialist counter-terrorism tactics require the adoption of specific training arrangements for its officers. The proposal therefore provides for the possibility of refraining from complying with the rules and principles of health and safety of the service with all safeguards available under the relevant conditions. This will be possible if a police officer of the counter-terrorism service finds that there is a likelihood of saving human life or reversing a threat to the security of the State when carrying out his duties in circumstances justified by a higher degree of necessity. This provision will allow the use of all means to save lives by counter-terrorism police officers. A similar solution already exists in the laws governing the activities of services such as Internal Security Agency, Intelligence Agency, Central Anti-corruption Office, Military Counterintelligence Service and Military Intelligence Service. Following the introduction of this solution, it was considered appropriate to define the provisions of the Police Act by unambiguously assigning to the Service Superior responsibility for ensuring appropriate safety and hygiene conditions for professional training or improvement services, in particular through direct coercion measures, as well as personal protective equipment or firearms. The precise health and safety conditions of the service during the course of training or professional improvement will be laid down in the implementing act of the Act. This will apply not only to the operation of the counter-terrorism service but also to all police officers. This will ensure appropriate training conditions throughout the formation, taking into account the specific features of direct coercive measures, personal protective equipment or firearms. Legal doubts regarding the responsibility of the superior for the organisation of vocational training or improvement will also be eliminated. This will ensure a high level of security for police officers, which is very important during training. Making the proposed changes, their introduction by the High Sejm is envisaged under the current permanent status of the Anti-Terror Operation Bureau of the Police Headquarters and the independent units of the anti-Terrorist Police. Their introduction will therefore not entail any additional burden on the state budget. However, it will significantly improve the organisational structure of police counter-terrorist units and make them more efficient. So these changes are actually organisational and do not entail additional costs, additional jobs. High Izbo! I ask for a positive assessment of the solutions presented in the project. I would like to stress the importance of the proposed legislation for the effectiveness of police counter-terrorism units, with the result that you will be able to respond to terrorist incidents and threats. The newly established service will complete the operation so far isolated within the Police Service, and I would remind you that today we have in the Police services: criminal, investigative, internal affairs, preventive and supporting police activities. This next counter-terrorist service will arrive as a separate police force. Until now, the Bureau of Anti-Terror Operations of the Police Headquarters and the police's own anti-terrorist units have functioned as preventive services, which did not correspond to the specific scope of their tasks and the special tactics used by the officers of these units. This case seems obvious. The proposed changes will ensure uniform preparation for response to terrorist collisions across the country based on an improved chain of command. High Seym! I am convinced that the introduction of these rules will ensure greater efficiency in police operations in situations requiring this. In order to warn the questions, I will say that it is not so that we are currently observing some special threats of a terrorist nature in Poland, but we need to prepare solutions that may be - hopefully never - necessary. Therefore, the High House is very much asking for support, preferably above the political divisions of this project. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u586	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club, '15 I am pleased to express my opinion on the draft law amending the Police Act and some other laws. The aim of the proposed amendment, as justified, is to ensure effective mechanisms for police organisational units carrying out counter-terrorism tasks. This would be done by adopting optimal organisational solutions, a single chain of command and mechanisms to ensure effective disposal of the forces and resources. This scope of tasks is currently being carried out by independent police anti-terrorist units: the Office of Anti-Terror Operations of the Police Headquarters and independent police counter-terrorism units. Attention is drawn to the fact that the way counter-terrorism works is in many respects different from the way in which criminal, investigative and preventive services are carried out, hence the need to separate police anti-terrorist units from the preventive service and to create counter-terrorist services. The newly established service is to consist of the Central Counter-Terrorist Division of the BOA Police and independent units of counter-terrorist police responsible for counter-terrorism activities within the meaning of the Act of 10 June 2016 on Anti-Terrorism and support of police agencies, including. under special risk conditions. The proposed amendments were envisaged within the framework of the current permanent status of the Office for Anti-Terrorism Operations of the Central Command and the independent anti-terrorist sub-groups. The designer also left the name BOA on the grounds of maintaining historical continuity and its high recognition abroad. The bill provides for the assignment of a new formation of competence in the field of coordination, preparation and implementation of counter-terrorism activities in the Police, which means inter alia. the substantive supervision of combat and training activities and the use of independent counter-terrorist sub-groups. The proposed amendment is the next step following the changes introduced in 2016, which extended police tasks to counter-terrorism activities. The draft submitted provides, inter alia, for this purpose. non-compliance with the rules and safety rules in situations where there is a higher need to save human life or to reverse the threat to State security, following the arrangements governing the functioning of other services, i.e. ABW, AW, CBA etc. The commander of the BOA will be appointed by the chief police officer, and the commander of the independent counter-terrorist divisions by the competent voivodeship commanders or the chief of the capital police. The proposed changes are not covered by European Union law. Ladies and gentlemen! This is an important project because it concerns an extremely sensitive sphere. Indeed, a kind of consolidation of dispersed counter-terrorism groups seems right. This can be a good direction for better and more effective action in this field. And this efficiency is the most important thing here. However, it is also important that these structural measures should be followed by measures aimed not only at maintaining the existing establishment, but also at increasing it and, which is very important, money. Therefore, the proposed bill should be discussed in detail in the framework of the work of the committee and even the subcommittee, so that the legislative effect is as effective as possible as an anti-terrorist tool. But since we're talking about the police today, there's no way to move on to the huge problems that policemen are facing. To this day, the low-income case in the police force has not been resolved. To this day, there have been no reservations raised as to the elements of the pension system or the so-called system. L4 in the case of police officers. To this day, print 2185, i.e. the Senate's draft amendment to the Police Act, which illegally discharged officers, cleared by the General Court, grants civil rights: restoration to work and related financial consequences. These are the police's most pressing problems. They're the ones the strikers want to talk about. It's their solution I'm asking for, it just seems to be a cry of a deaf man in the wilderness. If we don't solve these cases as a matter of urgency, there's no structural reform. He won't give us, because there won't be people willing to serve in these structures. I appeal to the government to address these issues seriously and urgently.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u587	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position towards the government's draft law amending the Police Act and some other laws. The aim of this project is to harmonise and streamline the system of operation of counter-terrorist troops in Poland. The novel provides for the separation from existing structures in the Central Police of the Counter-Terrorist Police Department of the BOA and 17 independent units throughout the country. Projectors assume that the established unit will deal with the fight against terrorism in Poland as a whole. New regulations on the subject of the novel are needed. I therefore welcome many of the proposed changes. In my opinion, it is important that, according to the new regulations, the Central Counter-Terrorist Unit of the Police will manage 17 independent units. He has used them only so far, and with the permission of the provincial commanders. I am convinced that such a change will improve the way the whole individual works, which will increase the security of citizens. I have no doubt that the improvement of the effectiveness of the BOA will also contribute to the harmonisation of the anti-terrorist training system, which is of particular importance in the case of large-scale action by several independent units at the same time. Above all, however, I am positively assessing the path of improving the professional police of counter-terrorism services. I welcome the fact that, under the new regulations, the commander of the BOA, his deputy and the commanders of the independent divisions will be appointed from among the officers of the counter-terrorism service. In my opinion, this solution will contribute to the efficiency of this unit and will ensure that persons with it managers will have appropriate experience and qualifications. Although there is little terrorist threat in Poland, I am glad that it is not trivialized. The bill - in my opinion - increases our security at no extra cost from the state budget and the liquidation of posts. About Free and Solidarni supports this bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u599	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Club15 on the government draft law on the circles of rural hostess. It is estimated that there are currently around 26 thousand in Poland. rural hostels with more than 1 million people. Rural hostels' circles carry out their mission in many areas, e.g. organize various kinds of events, promote folklore or cultivate local traditions, although the observation shows that unfortunately in recent times, without taking any merit or hard work, the rural housewives' circles function symbolically in many places, because it is not a definitive trend. This decline in interest in this form of action is probably due to the loss of institutional support and the lack of new legal regulations concerning rural hostels' circles, which would adjust the form of action of the rural hostel's circle to modern times. It should therefore be concluded that the development of wheels is often limited by the lack of formalisation of these organisations, which is to be countered by the proposed law. The main objective of the proposed law is primarily to formalise the functioning of the rural hostels' circles. This will be possible by entering a given rural housewives' circle in the National Register of Rural Housewives. This register will ultimately be kept by the development agency, ARiMR. Under the draft law, the Circle of Rural Houses will be raised to rank with the legal personality of self-governing social organisations of villagers independent of other socio-occupational organisations of farmers, as well as from government administration and local government units. The draft law also sets out the forms and principles of association in the circles of rural hostesses and the mode of establishing and organising internal wheels. Once entered in the register of wheels, it will acquire legal personality. This will solve many problems that rural hostels have been facing for years. Formization states that the possible application for financial grants will become realistic so far, which will certainly contribute to improving the functioning of the rural hostel circles. Such funding options are very important for the wheels, because even local authorities do not have the possibility to account for the financial support provided to rural housewives. The Kukiz Club15 submitted a similar bill aimed at regulating the situation of rural hostels. It is important for us that the provisions of this law will be implemented, no matter what politicians will be involved. For us, it is important that many of the problems faced by rural hostels' circles are solved. Finally, of course, there is a great danger that this law will become a tool for one party. I hope this doesn't happen. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u627	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! The draft law amending certain laws to facilitate the sale of food by farmers to shops and restaurants is a continuation of what worked out, but in a new form, with a view to a certain economic breakthrough, I think that also cultural, related to all the consequences that result from the dissemination of this form of management. There are two fundamental blocks of change that concern, first, tax issues. In the case of the possibility of tax-free sales processed in a way other than industrial, i.e. in domestic conditions, our own farm, plant and animal products, we raise the limit without tax from 20 to 40 thousand. £ per year. When this amount is exceeded, a low turnover tax of 2% will apply. Other provisions, which, of course, work completely complementaryly here, already apply to the scope of food law, since they provide, for example, that any processor who receives the income mentioned here will be entitled to benefit from tax exemptions or tax reliefs, provided that he uses at least 50% of his own raw material and does not employ employees. It is quite clear that at this point we are creating and very strengthening family farms, this way of management, this way of functioning of the rural family. Thanks to the solutions introduced by this law, in the amendment, tax facilitation will also be possible for those producers who sell their products via the Internet, which has not yet been possible. This is a response to expectations, and it is certainly an expression of development intuition, which is obvious, confirmed by the opinion and expectations of this solution. Another aspect of the case, also reported, also expected, concerns the extension of the catalogue of places to which processed food is made under household conditions. Here is a whole catalogue of recipients such as local restaurants, canteens, agrotourism farms, shops, other places with similar similar characteristics. Let's move on. This activity will only be subject to notification, registration by the Veterinary Inspection or the State Sanitary Inspection, depending on the nature, type of production, without the need for approval. Of course, this does not mean a disappearance or some indulgence in control. All the existing regulations, practices are here to ensure that it is healthy and high-quality food. ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u651	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The project is intended to create tax incentives for agricultural producers with additional income from the processing of domestic agricultural products. Allows inter alia. the use of a 2% flat-rate tax on recorded revenues from the sale of certain plant and animal products, processed in a non-industrial way, and increases the amount of income exempt from PIT to 40 000. zł. On the basis of which an amount of 40,000 was established. Evil? Shouldn't such legislation go a step further and set the amount of the exemption at 60,000. Evil? ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u652	The government draft law amending certain laws to facilitate the sale of food by farmers to shops and restaurants is another project in the package of today's agricultural laws, which aim to facilitate the sale of the food they produce to farmers. Farmers had to wait until the Law and Justice election campaign to look forward to these changes. Art. 1 project introduces the possibility of hiring an employee at trade fairs, festivals and exhibitions, allowing for rational use of the possibility of such sales, for example through the possibility of participating in events taking place at the same time, as well as the combination of sales of products from more than one farmer. Importantly, the requirement setting out the place of sale has been removed, which will result in farmers using new forms of sales, such as online sales and deliveries directly to their homes. The amount of income exempt from personal income tax was also increased from 20 000. Up to 40,000. zł. This change is not significant as the tax was not often a significant barrier to the development of sales in the context of agricultural retail trade. The tax paid was usually a reliable statement of income when applying for credit. It was possible to benefit from a flat-rate tax of 2% on recorded revenues from the sale of certain plant and animal products processed in a non-industrial manner. The Food Safety and Nutrition Act of 25 August 2006 proposes changes which will allow the farmer to produce and sell food not only directly to the consumer but also to retail establishments intended for the final consumer, namely commercial and catering establishments. Also art. 44a has been modified so that it is possible for the farmer to dispose of food to retail establishments if those establishments are located in the voivodship in which the production of that food takes place. This introduces a territorial restriction, which is justified because agricultural retail trade is supposed to be a form of local sales. The proposed changes are positive and, we hope, will make an essential contribution to the development of processing on agricultural holdings and processing in general. We also want simplified agricultural retail trade to be the first step towards the creation of larger plants on a general basis and to allow us to gain experience and to learn about the market. The content of the provisions is expected by farmers and will allow the development of processing on agricultural holdings, so the Modern Posel Club will vote in favour of them.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-10-03-sejm-69-2.u653	There is a large number of farmers who are interested in the so-called. small processing on their farms. This creates opportunities to generate additional income and improve the economic situation of farmers. This project creates legal incentives, including tax incentives, for those wishing to undertake such manufacturing. It also offers the possibility of selling homemade food to shops, cafés or restaurants. Our club supports all initiatives aimed at improving the very difficult situation of Polish farms today. This is what we will do in the vote.

The declaration is a kind of memorandum of understanding with very general records, it has a strict political and symbolic meaning, without changing in any way the legal order of both countries. At the same time, I kindly inform you that the Act of 27 June 2018 on the amendment of the Act on the Institute of National Remembrance - the Commission for the Investigation of Crimes Against the Nationals of Poland was not the subject of negotiations. Its adoption in this form was a sovereign decision of the constitutional legislative authority. This is how interpellations and parliamentary inquiries are treated. Undersecretary of State either - I apologize for the words - fucking stupid, or he considers Members to be idiots and does not answer the simplest questions. His answer is in clear contradiction with what was said by the representatives of the Law and Justice themselves, who indicated that this amendment was the subject of negotiations, and in fact it was in agreement with a foreign country. The case is appalling, because we have already faced a very rare situation in which changes in law in Poland are made under the influence of foreign factors, other countries, and this is still in secret at the headquarters of foreign interviews. Ladies and gentlemen! If this is the case with questions and parliamentary interpelations, it means that the government does not really care about legislative power. At this point, I would like to state that in the future this matter will certainly be resolved and certainly not left like this. Thank you very much.
